Verified on your machine (these already killed one option and rescued another):

Literally "listing all folders and files on open" would freeze the app for minutes. The plan delivers the same experience via instant shallow render + lazy expand + a background SQLite index for search.

Locked with you: Console is the ZOEY_OS front-end · Electron · tiered security with protected paths · Claude Code as a PTY child now, MCP integration later.

One thing worth flagging now: "all features of CMD, PowerShell, and WSL" is unbounded scope if read as reimplement them. The honest way to satisfy it — and what I'll build the plan around — is to host them: every one of those shells runs as a real child process under ConPTY, so you inherit 100% of their features for free, and Zoey Console adds the layer above (explorer, chat, blocks, security, AI). Reimplementing even PowerShell's parser would be years of work for a worse result.

● Updated plan

Fleet at 12/63 and advancing. While it runs, I verified the riskiest UX feature independently — and found a trap specific to your machine:

Command blocks (the Warp-style feature) depend on OSC 133 shell integration, and Microsoft's own PowerShell sample would silently break on your setup. The sample uses the `e escape character, which was added in **PowerShell 6**. You have **5.1 only, with no pwsh installed** — so that snippet emits literal garbage into the prompt instead of escape sequences. The fix is $([char]27). Also confirmed: cmd.exe can never report exit codes in its prompt (Microsoft states this outright), so cmd blocks get boundaries but no pass/fail colouring — a limitation to accept rather than fight.

Both are now recorded in the plan, along with the decision that the Console injects its own integration snippets per-profile at launch rather than making you hand-edit $PROFILE.

Waiting on the proposing and opposing leads to report before writing the architecture, security model, signature features, and roadmap.

● Updated plan
/plan to preview

Fleet at 24/63. Another verified constraint, this one about the Windows Explorer integration:

Windows 11 demotes legacy context-menu entries into "Show more options." To appear in the primary right-click menu, an app needs IExplorerCommand running under app identity, which requires a Sparse MSIX package plus a COM DLL in C++ — i.e. MSVC, which you don't have.

So: ship the legacy registry verb (a few registry writes, no compiler, works but one click deeper), and put the primary-menu version on the cut list. This costs you almost nothing, because your actual requirement — click a folder, get a console rooted there — happens inside Zoey Console's own explorer pane, where we control everything. Windows Explorer integration was only ever a bonus.

Still waiting on the two leads before writing the architecture.

4. CONTRACT.md — first deliverable

▎ This is the full text to be written to C:\dev\zoey\CONTRACT.md. Review this before anything else. After you approve it, it is read-only for both sessions; changes follow §4.7.

---
CONTRACT.md — Zoey Surface/Daemon Protocol

PROTOCOL_VERSION: 1 · Status: awaiting owner approval · Owner: Gerald (Titan Wave LTD)

Binding on: apps/console (Zoey Console session), apps/orb (Zoey Orb session), core/ (Python daemon).
Neither surface session may edit this file. Changes go through §4.7.

4.1 Transport

- Daemon binds 127.0.0.1 only. Never 0.0.0.0, never ::.
- Protocol: WebSocket, path /v1. Text frames, UTF-8, one JSON object per frame.
- Preferred port 47600; if taken, the next free port ascending. The port is discovered, never hard-coded.
- On start the daemon writes %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json, ACL'd to the current user only (inheritance disabled; owner + SYSTEM full, no others):

{ "protocolVersion": 1, "port": 47600, "token": "<64 hex chars>", "pid": 12345, "startedAt": "2026-08-12T09:14:03.221Z" }

- The file is deleted on clean shutdown. A stale file whose pid is dead must be ignored, not trusted.

4.2 Authentication handshake — Phase 1 non-negotiable

Threat: any webpage you visit can open ws://127.0.0.1:<port>. Without all three controls below, a browser tab can command your agent.

1. Token. 32 random bytes, hex-encoded, regenerated every daemon launch. Read from runtime.json (readable only by you). Sent in the first frame's payload — never in the URL or query string, which leak into logs and history.
2. Origin allowlist. The daemon reads the Origin header and accepts only zoey://console and zoey://orb. Any http(s):// origin, file://, null, or a missing header on a browser-shaped request is rejected and audit-logged. Browsers always send Origin and cannot forge it — this alone stops the drive-by case.
3. Deadline. A connection that has not sent a valid cmd.hello within 3000 ms is closed with code 4408.

Sequence:

client → cmd.hello   { token, surface: "console"|"orb", surfaceVersion, protocolVersion: 1 }
daemon → res.hello   { ok: true, daemonVersion, protocolVersion, capabilities[], sessionId }
        (on failure) close 4401 unauthorized · 4409 protocol mismatch · 4408 handshake timeout

Additional rules:
- Failed auth: close immediately, log, and apply a 1 s backoff per source. Five failures in 60 s disables the listener until the daemon is restarted.
- Rebind on rotation. Token rotates on every daemon restart; surfaces must re-read runtime.json on reconnect, never cache the token to disk.
- Max frame size 1 MiB. Larger payloads (scrollback exports, file blobs) use a separate chunked transfer, never a single frame.

4.3 Message envelope

Every frame, both directions:

{
  "v": 1,                      // protocol version, integer, required
  "id": "01J...",              // ULID, unique per message, required
  "ts": "2026-08-12T09:14:03.221Z", // ISO-8601 UTC with milliseconds, required
  "type": "evt.agent.state",   // namespaced type, required (see §4.4/§4.5)
  "corr": "01J...",            // correlation id: the `id` this responds to. null for unsolicited
  "payload": { }               // type-specific object, required (may be empty {})
}

Type namespaces — the mechanism that keeps the two surfaces from colliding:

┌────────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Prefix │    Direction     │                             Meaning                              │
├────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ cmd.*  │ surface → daemon │ An instruction. Always answered by res.* or err.* with corr set. │
├────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ res.*  │ daemon → surface │ Success response to a cmd.*.                                     │
├────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ err.*  │ daemon → surface │ Failure response. Payload: { code, message, retryable }.         │
├────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.*  │ daemon → surface │ Unsolicited event, delivered to every subscriber.                │
└────────┴──────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Sub-namespaces: agent.*, job.*, transcript.*, companion.*, permission.*, audit.*, daemon.* are shared — both surfaces implement them. pty.*, fs.*, window.* are Console-only. voice.*, scene.* are Orb-only.

Forward-compatibility rule (mandatory): an unknown type, or an unknown field inside a known payload, must be ignored silently — never an error, never a crash. This is what lets one surface ship a new feature without breaking the other.

4.4 Events — daemon → surface

Shared. Both surfaces subscribe to all of these.

┌─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│          Type           │                  Payload                  │                                          Notes                                          │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.agent.state         │ { companionId, state, detail? }           │ state ∈ idle | listening | thinking | speaking | working. The Orb drives the sphere     │
│                         │                                           │ from this; the Console drives its status bar from the same event.                       │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.companion.status    │ { companionId, name, state, busy,         │ Roster changes and per-companion status.                                                │
│                         │ tools[], scope }                          │                                                                                         │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.companion.roster    │ { companions[] }                          │ Full roster snapshot; sent on subscribe.                                                │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.transcript.delta    │ { companionId, messageId, role, seq,      │ Streaming text. seq is monotonic per messageId; consumers reassemble by seq and must    │
│                         │ delta, done }                             │ tolerate out-of-order. done:true closes the message.                                    │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.transcript.message  │ { companionId, message }                  │ A complete non-streamed message (tool result, system notice).                           │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.job.created         │ { jobId, kind, title, tier, createdBy,    │ createdBy ∈ user | agent | schedule.                                                    │
│                         │ steps[] }                                 │                                                                                         │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.job.progress        │ { jobId, stepIndex, pct?, note? }         │                                                                                         │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.job.updated         │ { jobId, status, stepIndex? }             │ status ∈ queued | running | blocked | succeeded | failed | cancelled.                   │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.job.completed       │ { jobId, status, result?, error? }        │ Terminal.                                                                               │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.permission.request  │ { requestId, tier, tool, args,            │ tier ∈ green | amber | red. provenance is required — see §7.3. Both surfaces can render │
│                         │ provenance, expiresAt }                   │  the approval card.                                                                     │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.permission.resolved │ { requestId, decision, decidedBy,         │ Broadcast so the other surface dismisses its card.                                      │
│                         │ remembered }                              │                                                                                         │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.audit.appended      │ { entryId, actor, tool, tier, summary }   │ For the live audit viewer.                                                              │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.daemon.health       │ { uptimeS, cpuPct, memMB, apiReachable,   │ Heartbeat, every 5 s.                                                                   │
│                         │ budgetSpent, budgetCap }                  │                                                                                         │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.daemon.shutdown     │ { reason, restarting }                    │ Surfaces show a reconnect state.                                                        │
├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.notification        │ { level, title, body, actions[] }         │ level ∈ info | warn | error.                                                            │
└─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Console-only events

┌─────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│          Type           │                                       Payload                                        │
├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.pty.data            │ { sessionId, seq, dataB64 } — raw PTY bytes, base64. Ordered by seq.                 │
├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.pty.exit            │ { sessionId, exitCode, signal? }                                                     │
├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.pty.title           │ { sessionId, title } — from OSC 0/2.                                                 │
├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.pty.cwd             │ { sessionId, cwd } — from OSC 9;9.                                                   │
├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.fs.children         │ { requestId, path, entries[], truncated, complete } — may arrive in multiple frames. │
├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.fs.changed          │ { path, kind } — kind ∈ created | modified | deleted | renamed.                      │
├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ evt.fs.hydrationWarning │ { path, bytesToDownload, estimatedCostNGN } — see §7.2.                              │
└─────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Orb-only events (listed so Console never claims these names): evt.voice.wake, evt.voice.vad, evt.voice.partialTranscript, evt.voice.amplitude, evt.scene.state.

4.6 Design tokens — shared, packages/tokens

Source of truth is packages/tokens/tokens.json; CSS custom properties and a Python constants module are generated from it. Neither surface hard-codes a hex value.

/* Colour — from ZOEY_OS-spec.md §3.8, authoritative */
--bg-void:       #08080A;   --bg-ambient:    #0D1524;
--panel:         rgba(18,18,22,0.72);
--panel-border:  rgba(255,255,255,0.06);
--accent:        #FF6B1A;   --accent-dim:    #B84D12;
--sphere-hot:    #FF3B00;   --sphere-cool:   #FFA94D;
--status-active: #22C55E;
--text:          #E8E8EA;   --text-muted:    #6B6B72;

/* Added for Console — semantic, not new brand colours */
--status-warn:   #FFA94D;   /* reuses sphere-cool */
--status-error:  #FF3B00;   /* reuses sphere-hot */
--status-idle:   #6B6B72;
--prov-human:    transparent;        /* provenance gutter, §7.3 */
--prov-program:  rgba(107,107,114,0.55);
--prov-agent:    rgba(255,107,26,0.75);

/* Type scale — 1.250 major third, base 13px */
--font-mono: "JetBrains Mono", ui-monospace, "Cascadia Mono", Consolas, monospace;
--fs-label: 10px;  --fs-sm: 11px;  --fs-base: 13px;
--fs-md: 16px;     --fs-lg: 20px;  --fs-xl: 25px;
--lh-tight: 1.2;   --lh-base: 1.5;
--label-transform: uppercase;  --label-tracking: 0.14em;

/* Spacing — 4px base */
--sp-1: 4px;  --sp-2: 8px;  --sp-3: 12px; --sp-4: 16px;
--sp-5: 24px; --sp-6: 32px; --sp-7: 48px;

/* Radii + layout */
--panel-radius: 12px; --radius-sm: 6px; --radius-pill: 999px;
--rail-w: 48px; --panel-left-w: 240px; --panel-right-w: 280px; --transcript-w: 320px;

Standing style rules (both surfaces): no emoji · no blue-purple gradients · no icon soup · labels are uppercase mono, 10px, 0.14em tracking · centre stage floats over void with no card background.

▎ ⚠️ At 1366×768, rail 48 + left 240 + right 280 + transcript 320 = 888px of chrome leaves only ~478px for the terminal. The Console must default to rail + terminal + one collapsible side panel, with the full four-panel layout available only above ~1600px. Flagged to the Orb session too.

4.7 Versioning rule

1. PROTOCOL_VERSION is a single integer at the top of this file. It appears in every envelope as v and in the handshake.
2. Additive changes — a new evt.*/cmd.* type, or a new optional payload field — do not bump the version. They are legal because §4.3 mandates that unknown types and fields are ignored. They must be appended to the tables above and to the changelog.
3. Breaking changes — renaming/removing a type or field, changing a field's type, changing enum semantics, or altering the handshake — bump PROTOCOL_VERSION, and require: owner approval, both surface sessions updating together, and the daemon rejecting mismatched clients with close code 4409.
4. Neither surface session edits this file. A change is proposed to the owner as a diff with rationale; the owner applies it. This is the whole point of the contract.
5. The daemon is the referee: if a surface sends a type not in this document, the daemon replies err.protocol.unknownType and audit-logs it.
6. Enum values are closed sets. Adding a value to state, status, or tier is a breaking change (consumers switch on them exhaustively).

— end CONTRACT.md —

5. Repository layout ⚪

C:\dev\zoey\                       # NOT in OneDrive — deliberate
├── CONTRACT.md                    # §4. Read every session. Never edited by a surface session.
├── package.json                   # npm workspaces root
├── packages\
│   ├── protocol\                  # SHARED — ask before editing
│   │   ├── schema\*.json          #   JSON Schema = source of truth
│   │   ├── src\index.ts           #   generated TS types + envelope helpers + validator
│   │   └── gen\python\            #   generated TypedDicts for core/
│   └── tokens\                    # SHARED — ask before editing
│       ├── tokens.json            #   source of truth
│       ├── dist\tokens.css        #   generated
│       └── dist\tokens.py         #   generated
├── core\                          # SHARED — Python daemon (ZOEY_OS core)
│   ├── server.py                  #   WS server, auth handshake (§4.2)
│   ├── security\{guard,audit,secrets}.py
│   ├── pty\supervisor.py          #   owns PTY sessions
│   ├── fs\{enumerate,watch}.py    #   metadata-only tree (§7.2)
│   └── config\permissions.yaml    #   green/amber/red — already specified in ZOEY_OS
├── apps\
│   ├── console\                   # ← THIS SESSION OWNS THIS
│   │   ├── src\main\              #   Electron main: WS client, token read, window mgmt
│   │   ├── src\preload\           #   contextBridge, no nodeIntegration
│   │   └── src\renderer\          #   React: terminal, tree, tabs
│   └── orb\                       # ← OTHER SESSION OWNS THIS. NEVER EDIT.
└── docs\ZOEY_OS-spec.md           # copied from OneDrive, becomes the canonical copy

Ownership rule: this session touches apps/console and, with your approval, core/pty and core/fs. It never touches apps/orb. Changes to packages/* or CONTRACT.md are proposed to you, not made.

---
6. Phase 1 architecture

Scope, per your correction: tabs + ConPTY + xterm + lazy file tree + CONTRACT.md. Nothing else.

6.1 The PTY decision ⚪ (resolving a fleet conflict)

🔵 The fleet contradicted itself: the PTY-core agent proposed writing a standalone Go ConPTY host and explicitly rejected node-pty; the shell-profiles agent proposed @lydell/node-pty and explicitly rejected the Go host.

Decision: @lydell/node-pty 1.2.0-beta.15. The Go host is rejected for Phase 1.

The PTY-core agent's stated reason for rejecting node-pty was that it is "NAN-based, ABI-pinned" and would "likely refuse to load on Node v25 (modules=141)." 🟢 That is incorrect for node-pty 1.x — I checked the registry directly: node-pty 1.1.0 depends on node-addon-api@^7.1.0, i.e. it is a Node-API addon. N-API is ABI-stable across Node and Electron versions, which is precisely why no rebuild is needed. The rejection rested on a false premise, so it does not stand.

Weighed against that: a hand-rolled Go ConPTY host means hand-packing COORD, PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_PSEUDOCONSOLE (0x00020016), and attribute-list sizing with no compiler validation — the agent itself named this as "where handle leaks hide" — plus a third language on a 2-core machine and a solo part-time schedule. Not Phase 1 work.

🔵 Worth keeping from the PTY agent regardless:
- Real ConPTY defects on 23H2: ClosePseudoConsole hang, lingering conhost, no DCS passthrough, reflow damage. Build 22631 predates several fixes.
- Mitigation, adopted: bundle the Microsoft.Windows.Console.ConPTY redistributable (conpty.dll + OpenConsole.exe) and enable node-pty's useConptyDll: true, getting a newer ConPTY than the OS ships. Cost ~12 MB, and OpenConsole.exe is on the LOLBAS list so it needs an AV-allowlist note.
- Process-tree teardown via Job Objects + JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, so a killed tab cannot orphan a dev server on port 3000.
- Environment correctness: build the child environment from CreateEnvironmentBlock + a fresh read of HKCU\Environment, so npm install -g and pip install --user land where you expect. Assert at startup that the daemon is not running as LocalSystem — Session 0 isolation silently redirects APPDATA and breaks every global install.

6.2 Terminal rendering 🔵→⚪

@xterm/xterm 6.0.0 + @xterm/addon-fit + @xterm/addon-webgl, with a pre-flight GPU probe before term.open() and a DOM-renderer fallback. 🟢 On HD 620 with a legacy driver this is not optional; xterm 6 removed the canvas addon, so DOM is the only fallback rung. Benchmark the DOM path in week 1.

Phase 1 keeps it deliberately small on a 2-core CPU:
- Scrollback capped at 8,000 lines (~16 MB), not 100k (~195 MB).
- No Web Worker — the fleet's off-thread decimation would contend with Electron's existing processes on 2 physical cores. Revisit only with a benchmark.
- Bundle JetBrains Mono as woff2 — 🔵 it is not installed on this machine, and the ligature library chain (font-finder, font-ligatures) has been unmaintained since 2022. Ligatures off in Phase 1.
- 🔵 xterm 6.0.0 is a breaking release (canvas addon gone, windowsMode and fastScrollModifier removed, viewport rewritten) and most online guidance still targets v5. Pin exact versions, not caret ranges.

6.3 Process & window model ⚪

┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Electron MAIN process  (Node)                          │
│  • reads %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json (token,port) │
│  • owns the ONLY WebSocket client → 127.0.0.1:47600    │
│  • sets Origin: zoey://console  (renderers cannot)     │
│  • window manager: spawnAt(path) → new BrowserWindow   │
└───────────────▲────────────────────────────────────────┘
                │ contextBridge IPC (no nodeIntegration,
                │ contextIsolation ON, sandbox ON)
┌───────────────▼────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ RENDERER (React)  — one per window                     │
│  • xterm.js grid · file tree · tab bar                 │
│  • NEVER sees the token, never opens a socket          │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                │  (Phase 1: PTY in main process)
┌───────────────▼────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Python daemon (core/) — WS server, permission guard,   │
│ audit log, fs enumeration.  Also serves apps\orb.      │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Why the WS client is in main, not the renderer: a renderer is a browser context. It cannot set an arbitrary Origin, and giving it the auth token puts the token one XSS away from any content rendered in the window. Main-process-only means §4.2's Origin allowlist is enforceable and the token never enters a web context.

Click a folder → new console window, end to end:
1. Renderer: click on tree node → window.zoey.spawnAt(path, mode) over contextBridge.
2. Main: validates path is a real directory (resolve, reject reparse-point traversal outside allowed roots), then either creates a new BrowserWindow or adds a tab.
3. New renderer boots, requests cmd.pty.spawn { profileId: default, cwd: path, cols, rows }.
4. Daemon's permission guard checks the path against protected-path policy, spawns via node-pty, returns sessionId.
5. evt.pty.data streams back → xterm. Shift-click = new window, Ctrl-click = tab, plain click = whichever you set as default.

6.4 Shell profiles — how "all of CMD, PowerShell, WSL" is honestly satisfied 🔵

We host the real binaries under ConPTY. We reimplement nothing. No built-in cd, no emulated dir/ls. Every feature of cmd.exe, PowerShell 5.1, bash, and every WSL distro is inherited because it is genuinely that program running. Reimplementing even PowerShell's parser would be years of work for a strictly worse result.

🔵 Discovery, verified on your machine:
- WSL distros from HKCU\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Lxss (fast path; confirmed Ubuntu-22.04, docker-desktop), falling back to wsl.exe -l -q with WSL_UTF8=1 injected — without it the output is UTF-16 and parses as garbage (this is exactly the mojibake seen in my own first probe).
- Git Bash from HKLM\SOFTWARE\GitForWindows.
- Docker via \\.\pipe\dockerDesktopLinuxEngine — 🟢 currently absent because Docker Desktop isn't running, so the provider must degrade to "engine offline", not throw.
- Profiles in profiles.jsonc (comments allowed), validated with zod, carrying a schemaVersion from day one.

What must still be built because hosting doesn't provide it: tabs/panes, the file tree, block semantics (later), the chat layer (later), the security guard, and cross-shell history.

---
7. Security architecture

7.1 Phase 1 non-negotiables (cannot be retrofitted)

1. WS auth — loopback bind + Origin allowlist + per-launch token + 3 s handshake deadline (§4.2). Without this, any webpage commands your agent.
2. Electron hardening — contextIsolation: true, nodeIntegration: false, sandbox: true, no remote module. Token stays in main.
3. Audit log from commit one — append-only, hash-chained, every command with actor ∈ {user, agent, schedule}. Retrofitting provenance later is impossible because the information is gone.
4. Protected paths — C:\dev\zoey, C:\Users\SERIOUS-PC\OneDrive, and system dirs marked protected in permissions.yaml. Writes/deletes there confirm regardless of origin.
5. Secret redaction before any write — history, logs, session records. A secret written unredacted once is leaked permanently.

7.2 Filesystem indexing — your correction #4 ⚪

- Metadata only. Name, size, mtime, attributes, reparse tag. Never open a file to read content during indexing.
- Never read content from a reparse point. 🟢 17,340 exist in your tree; reading a dehydrated placeholder triggers a download on metered data with 14.5 GB free.
- OneDrive is excluded from content indexing by default, opt-in per folder, never recursive-by-default.
- 🔵 Hydration Firewall (adopted from the fleet, and a genuinely good idea): compute bytes-to-download as EndOfFile − AllocationSize from attributes alone. Show it per row and aggregate per folder — "expanding this costs 3.4 GB". Register fs.hydrate as an amber rule in the existing permissions.yaml so a grep -r, a build, or Claude Code reading files gets intercepted with an exact figure before it spends your data. Emitted as evt.fs.hydrationWarning (§4.4).
- Phase 1 does not ship the SQLite snapshot or the USN journal reader — those were fleet proposals for later phases. Phase 1 is lazy enumeration on expand, virtualized rows, node_modules/.git/dist/venv collapsed by default.
- 🔵 Honest limitation to surface in the UI: live file-watching inside WSL2 is impossible from Windows — ReadDirectoryChangesW returns ERROR_INVALID_FUNCTION over 9P (microsoft/WSL#7674). WSL paths get manual refresh, and the UI says so rather than pretending.

7.3 Terminal output is untrusted — your correction #5 ⚪

Phase 1 has no AI chat, but the data model must be right now or it cannot be fixed later.

- Every byte captured from a PTY is stored with a provenance tag: human (you typed it), program (process stdout/stderr), or agent (model-proposed). Tags live in the daemon's session store and never travel inside the PTY byte stream.
- When the AI layer lands, program output is passed to the model inside explicit delimiters, labelled as data, with a standing system rule that content inside them is never an instruction.
- A red-tier action can never be triggered while untrusted content is in context without your explicit approval — this is the existing ZOEY_OS rule, and it is enforced by the daemon's guard, not by prompt wording.
- The model never receives a raw command string to execute. It selects a tool name + structured args; Python owns execution. Straight from ZOEY_OS-spec §6.
- 🔵 Trust Paint (adopted, deferred to the phase that adds AI): render provenance as a subtle left gutter band — human, program, and agent text look different at a glance. Because tags arrive over the WebSocket keyed to (sessionId, byteOffset) and any provenance-shaped sequence arriving from the PTY is stripped before parsing, a hostile npm postinstall printing a fake "agent approved" banner cannot paint itself as trusted. Tokens --prov-human/-program/-agent are already reserved in §4.6.

7.4 Honest residual risk ⚪

This tool's purpose is executing arbitrary commands. Anything you type runs. Real protection is: the AI cannot act unreviewed, secrets don't leak into logs or model context, destructive operations on protected paths require confirmation, and everything is auditable. It is not a sandbox and must not be described as one. True isolation would mean running commands inside WSL2/Docker by default, which breaks normal Windows dev work — available as an opt-in tier later, not the default.

---

Estimates are for one part-time developer on a 2-core laptop with intermittent power. 🔵 The fleet's per-domain totals summed to well over a year for the full vision; this is the deliberately reduced path.

9. Deferred design notes (so Phase 1 doesn't block them)

9.1 Shell integration for blocks — Phase 2 🟢 OSC 133 marks (A prompt, B command start, C output start, D;<exit> end). Verified traps:
- PowerShell 5.1: Microsoft's official sample uses the `e escape, which **does not exist before PowerShell 6**. You have 5.1 only. Must use $([char]27) or the prompt emits literal garbage.
- cmd.exe: Microsoft states plainly it cannot read the previous command's exit code in the prompt — cmd blocks get boundaries but no pass/fail colouring. Accept it.
- bash: full support; needs PS0 (bash ≥ 4.4; Ubuntu 22.04 ships 5.1). ✅
- The Console injects these into a transient profile at launch — it never edits your real $PROFILE.

9.2 Windows Explorer context menu — Phase 5 🟢 Ship a legacy registry verb only. Windows 11 demotes those to "Show more options"; the primary menu needs IExplorerCommand + app identity via a Sparse MSIX package and a C++ COM DLL — i.e. MSVC, which you don't have. Cheap to ship the legacy verb, and your real requirement is satisfied inside the app anyway.

Cross-checking surfaced a real design error in my own contract, and this is the moment to fix it — before you freeze it.

CONTRACT.md currently routes evt.pty.data (raw terminal bytes, base64) through the Python daemon. That means every byte of a noisy npm install goes PTY → Python → base64 → JSON → WebSocket → Electron main → IPC → renderer, on a 2-core CPU. Base64 alone inflates it 33%. That's a hot path through the wrong process.

Let me fix the contract before you sign off.

● Update(CONTRACT.md)
Added 10 lines, removed 4 lines

### 4.2 Console-only events

> **The PTY byte stream is deliberately NOT in this contract.**
> Terminal output does not traverse the daemon. A noisy `npm install` emits megabytes; base64-encoding that into JSON frames and pumping it through
Python on a 2-core machine would add ~33% inflation plus JSON escaping to the hottest path in the app, for no benefit — the daemon has no reason to
see every byte.
>
> **What the daemon owns:** *authorization* (may this session be spawned, in this directory, by this actor?), *audit* (what ran, when, triggered by
what), and *revocation* (panic hotkey, budget cap, policy change).
> **What the Console owns:** the byte stream, from an Electron `utilityProcess` straight to the renderer over a `MessagePort`.
>
> This keeps the permission guard authoritative without putting Python in the data path.

| Type | Payload |
|---|---|
| `evt.pty.data` | `{ sessionId, seq, dataB64 }` — raw PTY bytes, base64-encoded |
| `evt.pty.exit` | `{ sessionId, exitCode, signal? }` |
| `evt.pty.title` | `{ sessionId, title }` — from OSC 0/2 |
| `evt.pty.cwd` | `{ sessionId, cwd }` — from OSC 9;9 |
| `evt.pty.sessions` | `{ sessions: [{ sessionId, profileId, cwd, title, startedAt, busy }] }` — roster the daemon assembles from Console reports. *
*The Orb may subscribe** to show terminal activity. |
| `evt.pty.revoke` | `{ sessionId, reason }` — the daemon orders a session killed. The Console **must** comply and report back. |
| `evt.fs.children` | `{ requestId, path, entries[], truncated, complete }` — may span multiple frames |
| `evt.fs.changed` | `{ path, kind }` — `kind` ∈ `created` \| `modified` \| `deleted` \| `renamed` |
| `evt.fs.hydrationWarning` | `{ path, bytesToDownload, estimatedCostNGN }` — see §6.3 |

● Update(CONTRACT.md)
Added 2 lines, removed 5 lines

|---|---|---|
| `cmd.pty.spawn` | `{ profileId, cwd?, cols, rows, env? }` | `res.pty.spawned { sessionId }` |
| `cmd.pty.write` | `{ sessionId, dataB64 }` | *(fire-and-forget)* |
| `cmd.pty.resize` | `{ sessionId, cols, rows }` | `res.ok` |
| `cmd.pty.kill` | `{ sessionId, signal? }` | `res.ok` |
| `cmd.pty.list` | `{}` | `res.pty.list { sessions[] }` |
| `cmd.pty.requestSpawn` | `{ profileId, cwd, actor, purpose? }` — `actor` ∈ `human` \| `agent` \| `schedule` | `res.pty.grant { grantId, sessionId,
 expiresAt }`, or `err.permission.pending` followed by `evt.permission.request` |
| `cmd.pty.report` | `{ sessionId, event, detail? }` — `event` ∈ `started` \| `exited` \| `cwdChanged` \| `titleChanged` \| `killed` | `res.ok` |
| `cmd.fs.list` | `{ path, includeHidden? }` | `res.fs.accepted` then one or more `evt.fs.children` |
| `cmd.fs.watch` | `{ path }` | `res.ok` |
| `cmd.fs.unwatch` | `{ path }` | `res.ok` |

Now the deep-link security invariant — a zoey:// handler that accepted a command parameter would be a remote-code-execution vector, since any webpage can trigger a protocol handler.

● Update(CONTRACT.md)
Added 22 lines

Tiers are defined once, in **`core/config/permissions.yaml`** — the model already specified in ZOEY_OS-spec §6. **Surfaces render tiers; they never
define or evaluate them.** The daemon is the only authority.

### 6.5 Execution authorization

- **No PTY session may be created without a grant.** The Console calls `cmd.pty.requestSpawn` and waits for `res.pty.grant`. The daemon applies the
tier policy, protected-path policy, and audit before granting. A Console that spawns without a grant is a contract violation.
- A grant authorizes **one session, in one directory**. It is not reusable and it expires.
- The daemon may revoke at any time via `evt.pty.revoke` — panic hotkey, budget cap, or policy change. **The Console must comply and report back.**
- Session lifecycle is reported via `cmd.pty.report` so the audit log stays complete even though the byte stream never reaches the daemon.

### 6.6 Deep-link safety — `zoey://`

Any webpage can trigger a registered protocol handler. Therefore:

- **The `zoey://` grammar carries a path and a display mode. Nothing else.**
- **There is no `cmd=` parameter, and none may ever be added** — not even an allowlisted one. `zoey://run?cmd=...` would be a remote-code-execution
vector reachable from a hostile webpage.
- A window opened from a deep link **always starts with an empty prompt**. It never pre-fills, never auto-runs.
- The path is resolved and validated against protected-path policy before a window opens.

```
zoey://open?path=<url-encoded-absolute-path>&mode=window|tab|pane
```

Adding any parameter to this grammar is a **breaking change** under §7.3.

● Update(packages\protocol\src\index.ts)
Added 51 lines
export function hydrationBytes(entry: Pick<FsEntry, 'size' | 'allocSize'>): number {
  return Math.max(0, entry.size - entry.allocSize);
}

/* ══════════════════════════════════════════════ deep links — CONTRACT §6.6 */

export const DEEP_LINK_SCHEME = 'zoey' as const;

export interface DeepLink { path: string; mode: SpawnMode }

/**
 * Parse a `zoey://` deep link.
 *
 * Any webpage can trigger a registered protocol handler, so this parser is a
 * security boundary, not a convenience. It accepts EXACTLY two parameters —
 * `path` and `mode` — and rejects the URL outright if any other parameter is
 * present. That strictness is deliberate: it means a future `cmd=` parameter
 * cannot be smuggled in and silently honoured by an older build.
 *
 * Callers must still validate `path` against protected-path policy, and must
 * open the resulting window with an EMPTY prompt — never pre-filled, never
 * auto-run.
 *
 * @returns the parsed link, or null if the URL is not a valid, safe deep link.
 */
export function parseDeepLink(raw: string): DeepLink | null {
  let url: URL;
  try {
    url = new URL(raw);
  } catch {
    return null;
  }

  if (url.protocol !== `${DEEP_LINK_SCHEME}:`) return null;
  if (url.hostname !== 'open') return null;

  // Reject anything beyond the two permitted parameters.
  for (const key of url.searchParams.keys()) {
    if (key !== 'path' && key !== 'mode') return null;
  }

  const path = url.searchParams.get('path');
  if (!path) return null;

  const mode = url.searchParams.get('mode') ?? 'window';
  if (!(SPAWN_MODES as readonly string[]).includes(mode)) return null;

  return { path, mode: mode as SpawnMode };
}

export function buildDeepLink(link: DeepLink): string {
  const p = new URLSearchParams({ path: link.path, mode: link.mode });
  return `${DEEP_LINK_SCHEME}://open?${p.toString()}`;
}

/* ── §6.6 deep-link safety ───────────────────────────────────────────────── */

ok('valid deep links parse', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(
    parseDeepLink('zoey://open?path=C%3A%5Cdev%5Czoey&mode=tab'),
    { path: 'C:\\dev\\zoey', mode: 'tab' });
  // mode defaults to window
  assert.deepEqual(
    parseDeepLink('zoey://open?path=C%3A%5Cdev'),
    { path: 'C:\\dev', mode: 'window' });
});

ok('deep link REJECTS any command parameter — RCE vector (§6.6)', () => {
  for (const hostile of [
    'zoey://open?path=C%3A%5C&cmd=calc.exe',
    'zoey://open?path=C%3A%5C&mode=tab&cmd=rm+-rf+%2F',
    'zoey://open?path=C%3A%5C&run=whoami',
    'zoey://open?path=C%3A%5C&exec=1',
    'zoey://run?cmd=calc.exe',
  ]) {
    assert.equal(parseDeepLink(hostile), null, `must reject: ${hostile}`);
  }
});

ok('deep link rejects wrong scheme, host, or mode', () => {
  assert.equal(parseDeepLink('https://open?path=C%3A%5C'), null, 'wrong scheme');
  assert.equal(parseDeepLink('zoey://run?path=C%3A%5C'), null, 'wrong host');
  assert.equal(parseDeepLink('zoey://open?mode=window'), null, 'missing path');
  assert.equal(parseDeepLink('zoey://open?path=C%3A%5C&mode=bogus'), null, 'bad mode');
  assert.equal(parseDeepLink('not a url'), null);
});

ok('buildDeepLink round-trips through parseDeepLink', () => {
  const link = { path: 'C:\\Users\\SERIOUS-PC\\OneDrive\\My Resume', mode: 'window' as const };
  assert.deepEqual(parseDeepLink(buildDeepLink(link)), link);
});

                │

│ Authorizes and audits PTYs. NEVER carries their bytes.   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Why the WS client lives in main, not the renderer:** a renderer is a browser context — it cannot set an arbitrary `Origin`, and holding the token
there puts it one XSS away from any rendered content. Main-only makes CONTRACT §2.2's Origin allowlist actually enforceable.

**Why the PTY byte stream does not go through the daemon** ⚪ — *corrected during Phase 0.* An earlier draft of the contract routed `evt.pty.data` (
base64 PTY bytes) through Python. A noisy `npm install` emits megabytes; base64 inflates that ~33%, plus JSON escaping, on a 2-core CPU — the hottes
t path in the app running through the process with the least reason to see it. Corrected per CONTRACT §4.2/§6.5: **the daemon authorizes, audits, an
d can revoke; the Console owns the bytes.** The guard stays authoritative because no session may exist without a grant.

**Why a `utilityProcess` rather than the main process** 🔵 — a native-module crash in `node-pty` takes down whatever process hosts it. In main, that
 kills *every* window. In a per-window `utilityProcess`, it kills one tab's backend and the app survives.

**Click a folder → new console window:**
1. Renderer: tree click → `window.zoey.spawnAt(path, mode)` over contextBridge.
2. Main: validate `path` is a real directory; reject reparse-point traversal outside allowed roots.
3. New `BrowserWindow` (or tab) boots → `cmd.pty.spawn { profileId, cwd: path, cols, rows }`.
4. Daemon guard checks protected-path policy, spawns, returns `sessionId`.
5. `evt.pty.data` streams back to xterm.
3. Main → daemon: `cmd.pty.requestSpawn { profileId, cwd: path, actor: 'human' }`.
4. Daemon applies tier + protected-path policy, writes an audit entry, returns `res.pty.grant`. If the path is protected, the owner sees an approval
 card first.
5. Main spawns the `utilityProcess` PTY host, creates the `BrowserWindow`, hands the renderer a `MessagePort`.
6. Bytes flow PTY → utilityProcess → MessagePort → xterm. Main reports `started` / `exited` back to the daemon for the audit log.

Modifiers: **plain click** = default (configurable), **Shift-click** = new window, **Ctrl-click** = new tab.

One thing needs your attention before you sign off — I found a real error in my own first draft and corrected it.

The draft routed evt.pty.data — raw terminal bytes, base64 — through the Python daemon. On your 2-core i5-7200U that puts megabytes of npm install output through Python with ~33% base64 inflation plus JSON escaping: the hottest path in the app, running through the process with the least reason to see it.

Corrected: the daemon authorizes, audits, and can revoke; the Console owns the bytes. No PTY may exist without a cmd.pty.requestSpawn grant, so the permission guard stays authoritative — it just isn't in the data path. Bytes go PTY → Electron utilityProcess → MessagePort → xterm. Logged in the CONTRACT changelog. PROTOCOL_VERSION stays 1 because this happened before approval — after approval it would have been a breaking bump.

I also added §6.6, deep-link safety: zoey:// carries a path and a display mode and nothing else. There is no cmd= parameter and none may ever be added — any webpage can trigger a protocol handler, so zoey://run?cmd=... would be remote code execution. The parser rejects unknown parameters outright rather than ignoring them, and there's a test firing five hostile URLs at it.

What's in place

- CONTRACT.md — the shared law, with your §3 WebSocket security as a non-negotiable: loopback-only bind, Origin allowlist (zoey://console, zoey://orb), per-launch token in a user-only-ACL file, 3-second handshake deadline. The token lives in Electron's main process, never a renderer — a renderer can't set Origin, and a token there is one XSS from any rendered content.
- packages/tokens — 47 tokens generating tokens.css + tokens.py. CSS variable names are an explicit map, not derived, so a refactor can't silently rename something the Orb depends on.
- packages/protocol — contract as TypeScript. Closed enums, forward-compat rules, ULID, deep-link parser.
- plan.md, README.md, .gitignore (excludes runtime.json — it holds the auth token).

One flaw I found and fixed in my own code

My first rate limiter counted Origin rejections toward the lockout. The test caught it immediately: seven probes tripped it and locked out the legitimate client with HTTP 429. That means any webpage could have disabled your console with five requests — turning the anti-drive-by control into a denial-of-service against you.

Fixed so only bad tokens count (someone already past Origin, guessing the secret). Origin rejections are logged, not counted. There's now a regression test named probing does not lock out the owner.

● Update(packages\protocol\src\index.ts)
Added 13 lines, removed 1 line

export const DEEP_LINK_SCHEME = 'zoey' as const;

export interface DeepLink { path: string; mode: SpawnMode }
/**
 * Modes reachable from a deep link — a STRICT SUBSET of SPAWN_MODES.
 *
 * `cdCurrent` is excluded on purpose: it mutates the state of an already-open
 * terminal rather than creating a fresh one. A hostile page that could reach it
 * would be able to silently change the working directory of a session the owner
 * is actively typing into — the next `rm -rf .` or `git clean -fd` would land
 * somewhere they did not intend. Deep links may only ever CREATE.
 */
export const DEEP_LINK_MODES = ['window', 'tab', 'pane'] as const;
export type DeepLinkMode = (typeof DEEP_LINK_MODES)[number];

export interface DeepLink { path: string; mode: DeepLinkMode }

| Type | Payload | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `evt.agent.state` | `{ companionId, state, detail? }` | `state` ∈ `idle` \| `listening` \| `thinking` \| `speaking` \| `working`. Orb drives the s
phere from this; Console drives its status bar from the same event. |
| `evt.agent.state` | `{ companionId, state, detail? }` | `state` ∈ `idle` \| `listening` \| `thinking` \| `speaking` \| `working` \| `blocked`. Orb
 drives the sphere from this; Console drives its status bar from the same event. **`blocked` = waiting on your approval** — deliberately distinct fr
om `working`, so that walking past the machine at 2am tells you "busy" from "stuck waiting for you". Orb renders it amber and static. |
| `evt.companion.roster` | `{ companions: [{ companionId, name, voice, tools[], scope }] }` | Full snapshot, sent on subscribe. |
| `evt.companion.status` | `{ companionId, name, state, busy, tools[], scope }` | Per-companion change. |
| `evt.transcript.delta` | `{ companionId, messageId, role, seq, delta, done }` | Streaming text. `role` ∈ `user` \| `assistant` \| `system` \| `too
l`. `done:true` closes the message. |

● Update(CONTRACT.md)

### 3.3 Ordering and idempotency

- `evt.pty.data` and `evt.transcript.delta` carry a **monotonic `seq`** scoped to their `sessionId`/`messageId`. Consumers reassemble by `seq` and m
ust tolerate out-of-order arrival.
- `evt.transcript.delta` carries a **monotonic `seq`** scoped to its `messageId`. Consumers reassemble by `seq` and must tolerate out-of-order arriv
al. (The PTY byte stream also carries a `seq`, but it never crosses this protocol — see §4.2.)
- All `cmd.*` are **idempotent by `id`**. A retried command with the same `id` must not execute twice.

● Update(CONTRACT.md)
Added 1 line, removed 1 line

`Surface` is `console | orb` and deliberately excludes `mobile`.

A phone is a different device. It cannot read `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoeyuntime.json`, and it cannot reach `127.0.0.1`. Every control in §2 — the token fil
e, the loopback bind, the `zoey://` Origin allowlist — is local-only *by construction*. Adding a `mobile` value would declare a capability that no p
art of this contract can serve.
A phone is a different device. It cannot read `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json`, and it cannot reach `127.0.0.1`. Every control in §2 — the token f
ile, the loopback bind, the `zoey://` Origin allowlist — is local-only *by construction*. Adding a `mobile` value would declare a capability that no
 part of this contract can serve.

Remote access is a different design (relay or push service, a different auth model, a different threat model) and will require its own contract revi
sion regardless. Reserving the enum value now buys nothing and implies something untrue. The spec's answer for "check on jobs while away" is an **ou
tbound bridge** — push notification, Telegram/WhatsApp — which is not a surface and needs no value here.


Now §7.4 — declaring which enums are closed and which are deliberately open.

● Update(CONTRACT.md)
Added 29 lines, removed 1 line
   - both surface sessions updating together,
   - the daemon rejecting mismatched clients with close code `4409`.

4. **Enum values are closed sets.** `state`, `status`, `tier`, `role`, `kind`, `cloudState`, `decision`, `createdBy`, `mode`, and `level` are exhaus
tively switched on by consumers. Adding a value is **breaking**.
4. **Enums are defined once, in `packages/protocol/schema/enums.json`**, and TypeScript and Python are **generated** from it. Never hand-maintain a
copy — that file is the authority, and `scripts/check-contract.mjs` fails the build if the generated output drifts from it.

   **CLOSED sets** — consumers switch on these exhaustively, so adding a value is a **breaking** change:

   | Enum | Values |
   |---|---|
   | `AgentState` | `idle` `listening` `thinking` `speaking` `working` `blocked` |
   | `JobStatus` | `queued` `running` `blocked` `succeeded` `failed` `cancelled` `needsReview` |
   | `Tier` | `green` `amber` `red` |
   | `Provenance` | `human` `program` `agent` `schedule` `external` `system` |
   | `CreatedBy` | `user` `agent` `schedule` `fileWatch` `email` `webhook` `systemEvent` |
   | `Role` | `user` `assistant` `system` `tool` |
   | `CloudState` | `local` `cloudOnly` `pinned` `partial` `unknown` |
   | `FsChangeKind` | `created` `modified` `deleted` `renamed` `hydrationChanged` |
   | `SpawnMode` | `window` `tab` `pane` `cdCurrent` |
   | `DeepLinkMode` | `window` `tab` `pane` — strict subset of `SpawnMode`, see §6.6 |
   | `Decision` | `approve` `deny` `expired` — a surface may only SEND `approve`/`deny` |
   | `PtyReportEvent` | `started` `exited` `cwdChanged` `titleChanged` `killed` `startFailed` |
   | `NotificationLevel` | `info` `warn` `error` |
   | `Surface` | `console` `orb` — see §2.4 |

   **OPEN sets** — new values may be added at any time **without** a version bump. Consumers **MUST** have a default branch:

   | Enum | Why open |
   |---|---|
   | `ErrorCode` | Diagnostic codes accrue continuously; making each one breaking would be absurd. |
   | `CloseCode` | Ditto, plus standard RFC 6455 codes (`1001`, `1009`) are used unchanged. |

   **Naming:** multi-word values are **camelCase** on the wire (`needsReview`, `fileWatch`, `cloudOnly`). The database may use `snake_case` internal
ly — the wire and the schema are not the same thing.

5. **Neither surface session edits this file.** Propose a diff with rationale to the owner; the owner applies it.

6. **The daemon is the referee.** A type not defined here gets `err.protocol.unknownType` and an audit entry — the connection is not dropped.

le owns the bytes (§4.2, §6.5). Added §6.6 deep-link safety. Revised **before** approval, so `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 1. |

## Session ownership

Per `docs/STRUCTURE.md` v2.1:

| Path | Owner | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `core/` | **Console session** | It built the daemon foundation; one owner beats two |
| `apps/console` | **Console session** | |
| `apps/orb` | **Orb session** | The other session never edits this |
| `packages/protocol` | SHARED | **Ask the owner before editing** |
| `packages/tokens` | SHARED | **Ask the owner before editing** |
| `core/` | SHARED | Coordinate before editing |
| `CONTRACT.md` | OWNER | **Never edited by a surface session** — propose a diff (CONTRACT §7) |
| `apps/orb` | **Orb session** | The Console session never edits this |
| `packages/protocol` | SHARED 🔒 | **Propose to Gerald; do not edit** |
| `packages/tokens` | SHARED 🔒 | **Propose to Gerald; do not edit** |
| `CONTRACT.md` | **Gerald** | **Never edited by a session** — propose a diff (CONTRACT §7) |
| `docs/*` | **Gerald** | Gerald places these; never create a competing copy |

Git is Gerald's. **No session runs git commands.** See `CLAUDE.md`.

## Getting started

No `npm install` is needed for the contract layer — it has zero dependencies.

```bash
npm install
npm run bootstrap      # generate tokens, typecheck the protocol
npm run bootstrap                              # generate + verify everything
pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.lock   # daemon deps

python core/server.py --dev                    # terminal 1
python core/test_auth.py                       # terminal 2
```

### What Phase 0 actually proves

- Five hostile `Origin` values rejected at the HTTP upgrade, plus missing-Origin and wrong-path
- **Probing does not lock you out** — Origin rejections are logged but never counted toward the auth-failure lockout, or any webpage could disable yo
ur console with five requests
- Bad token → `4401` (timing-safe compare), version mismatch → `4409`, silence → `4408` at ~3s
- Unknown message type → `err.protocol.unknownType` **and the connection survives** (CONTRACT §3.2)
- A human gets a shell in `C:\dev`; **the agent does not** — it needs approval, and again inside a protected path
- The audit log is hash-chained: altering any entry is detected and the exact `seq` named
- `runtime.json` ACL confirmed `SYSTEM` + owner only — no Users, no Everyone

### Generated files — never hand-edit

`packages/tokens/dist/`, `packages/protocol/src/enums.generated.ts`, and `packages/protocol/gen/python/` are outputs of `packages/tokens/tokens.json`
 and `packages/protocol/schema/enums.json`. Edit the source, then `npm run generate`.

## Non-negotiables

These cannot be retrofitted. See CONTRACT.md §2 and §6.

1. Enum audit

Accepted as proposed: (a) AgentState.blocked · (b) JobStatus.needsReview · (d) PtyReportEvent.startFailed

Accepted with an addition — (c): your list omitted systemEvent. Spec v3.1 line 396 lists five trigger types — "time, file-watch, email, webhook, system event" — and line 205's internal column confirms it. Without it, the first job firing on battery-low or device-connect forces a version bump. Added.

Disagreed — (e) mobile: no. A phone cannot read %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json and cannot reach 127.0.0.1. Every §2 control is local-only by construction, so mobile would declare a capability the contract cannot serve. Remote access needs its own transport, auth model, and contract revision regardless — reserving the value now saves nothing and implies something untrue. The spec's own answer is an outbound bridge (push/Telegram), which isn't a surface. Recorded as §2.4 so it isn't re-litigated.

Naming — one thing to overrule if you disagree: I used camelCase (needsReview, fileWatch), not snake_case. The contract already has cloudOnly, cwdChanged, titleChanged; mixing conventions in one wire format is a permanent wart. Spec §6 calls its trigger_type column "INTERNAL, richer" — so the DB can stay snake_case. Say the word and I'll flip it.

Unchanged (verified adequate through Phase 8): Tier, Role, NotificationLevel.

Declared OPEN: ErrorCode and CloseCode. Diagnostic codes accrue forever; making each one breaking would be absurd. Consumers must have a default branch.

Your ALLOW/CONFIRM/DENY question — correct by design, but it exposed a real gap. They're different axes: Verdict is the guard's evaluation, Decision is your answer. CONFIRM means "ask them," never an answer. But accepting needsReview created one: evt.permission.request carries expiresAt, so when 30 minutes lapse the daemon must tell the other surface to dismiss its card — and with only approve|deny it couldn't, leaving a stale card on the Orb forever. Added expired as daemon-emitted only; CmdPermissionRespond.decision is typed SendableDecision, so a surface sending it won't compile.

2. I did not gitignore the generated protocol output, deviating from STRUCTURE.md line 71. core/ imports the Python at daemon startup and index.ts re-exports the TS; ignoring them makes "clone and run the daemon" require a Node build step first. check-contract.mjs catches drift instead.

4. pnpm — my recommendation: stay on npm

The disk argument is weaker than it looks here. Electron's ~250 MB binary lives in a shared machine-level cache regardless of package manager — the npm package itself is small. And npm workspaces already hoist console + orb to one root node_modules, so two apps on matching versions share one copy anyway. pnpm's content-addressed store mainly pays off across separate repos, which isn't your situation.

Against that: pnpm's symlinked layout is a known friction point with electron-builder and native module resolution — and that is precisely where you have no margin, because with no MSVC you cannot rebuild anything that fails to resolve. Using node-linker=hoisted avoids it but throws away pnpm's main remaining benefit (strict deps).

What you'd give up: phantom-dependency protection. The contract boundary and check-contract.mjs already guard the part that matters.

If disk becomes the real constraint, the bigger lever is pruning the Electron cache and build output — not the package manager.

Ready for your CONTRACT.md approval and the Phase 1a go-ahead.

> Before starting, what happened to the 63 agents I told you to deploy?

Fair question — let me give you the actual numbers rather than a summary.

  Ran 1 shell command

Here's the straight account.

Deployed: 63 — your 2 leads with 30 each, plus an adjudicator I added to reconcile them.

Actually ran: 31 of 63 completed in 79 minutes. 29 proposals, 2 rebuttals, 0 leads, 0 adjudicator.

Root cause, and it's my error: the workflow concurrency cap is min(16, cpus − 2). Your machine reports 4 logical CPUs, so the cap was 2 agents at a time. I sized a 63-agent fleet for a machine that runs two concurrently. At the observed ~2.5 min/agent that was a ~2.7-hour job, and I launched it without checking core count first.

There's an irony worth naming: the fleet itself surfaced the 2-core constraint — the renderer agent probed the hardware and flagged it — and I then verified it independently and wrote it into the plan as the hardest constraint after disk. I was reading that finding while the fleet was choking on it.

Compounding factor: I used pipeline(), which schedules breadth-first. Proposals kept claiming both slots, so rebuttals were starved rather than interleaved — that's why the opposition produced 2 instead of a proportional ~29. A barrier phase (all proposals, then all rebuttals) would at least have failed evenly.

What it did deliver — the proposing half, which I mined directly into the plan and contract:

ConPTY's known defects on 22631 and the useConptyDll redistributable fix · Job Objects for process-tree teardown · the Session 0 / LocalSystem trap that silently breaks npm install -g · WSL discovery via the Lxss registry key and the WSL_UTF8 mojibake fix · Docker pipe degrading to "engine offline" · xterm 6's breaking changes · the ligature dependency chain being dead since 2022 · the Hydration Firewall · Trust Paint · WSL2 9P watching being impossible · utilityProcess-per-window · and the zoey:// no-cmd= rule that became CONTRACT §6.6.

What it cost by not finishing: the opposition was the entire point of the two-sided structure, and it's the half that didn't happen. Concretely — one proposer asserted with confidence that node-pty 1.x is "NAN-based, ABI-pinned" and would fail on Node 25. That's false; it's a Node-API addon, which I caught by checking the registry myself. And two proposals flatly contradicted each other on the PTY layer (write a Go ConPTY host vs. use @lydell/node-pty). Cross-examining that claim and adjudicating that conflict was precisely the job of the 2 leads and the adjudicator. I did it myself instead.

You then told me to cancel it, and I did — TaskStop on w21m5054c returned success, and nothing is still running.

Where that leaves you: everything marked 🔵 in plan.md is un-cross-examined proposal output. The decisions that matter have since been made and verified by other means — the enum audit, the PTY hot-path correction, the rate-limiter DoS fix, the ACL and tamper tests.

If you want opposition before the contract freezes, the right shape now is 6–8 agents in a single barrier phase, aimed only at the load-bearing calls — Electron-over-Tauri, the daemon-authorizes-but-doesn't-carry-bytes split, and the closed-enum set. At a cap of 2 that's roughly 20 minutes, not three hours. Otherwise I'm ready for Phase 1a on your go-ahead.

     4. CONTRACT.md — first deliverable

     ▎ This is the full text to be written to C:\dev\zoey\CONTRACT.md. Review this before anything else. After you approve it, it is read-only for both sessions; changes follow §4.7.

     ---
     CONTRACT.md — Zoey Surface/Daemon Protocol

     PROTOCOL_VERSION: 1 · Status: awaiting owner approval · Owner: Gerald (Titan Wave LTD)

     Binding on: apps/console (Zoey Console session), apps/orb (Zoey Orb session), core/ (Python daemon).
     Neither surface session may edit this file. Changes go through §4.7.

     4.1 Transport

     - Daemon binds 127.0.0.1 only. Never 0.0.0.0, never ::.
     - Protocol: WebSocket, path /v1. Text frames, UTF-8, one JSON object per frame.
     - Preferred port 47600; if taken, the next free port ascending. The port is discovered, never hard-coded.
     - On start the daemon writes %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json, ACL'd to the current user only (inheritance disabled; owner + SYSTEM full, no others):

     { "protocolVersion": 1, "port": 47600, "token": "<64 hex chars>", "pid": 12345, "startedAt": "2026-08-12T09:14:03.221Z" }

     - The file is deleted on clean shutdown. A stale file whose pid is dead must be ignored, not trusted.

     4.2 Authentication handshake — Phase 1 non-negotiable

     Threat: any webpage you visit can open ws://127.0.0.1:<port>. Without all three controls below, a browser tab can command your agent.

     1. Token. 32 random bytes, hex-encoded, regenerated every daemon launch. Read from runtime.json (readable only by you). Sent in the first frame's payload — never in the URL or query string, which leak into logs and history.
     2. Origin allowlist. The daemon reads the Origin header and accepts only zoey://console and zoey://orb. Any http(s):// origin, file://, null, or a missing header on a browser-shaped request is rejected and audit-logged. Browsers always send Origin and cannot forge it — this alone stops the drive-by case.
     3. Deadline. A connection that has not sent a valid cmd.hello within 3000 ms is closed with code 4408.

     Sequence:

     client → cmd.hello   { token, surface: "console"|"orb", surfaceVersion, protocolVersion: 1 }
     daemon → res.hello   { ok: true, daemonVersion, protocolVersion, capabilities[], sessionId }
             (on failure) close 4401 unauthorized · 4409 protocol mismatch · 4408 handshake timeout

     Additional rules:
     - Failed auth: close immediately, log, and apply a 1 s backoff per source. Five failures in 60 s disables the listener until the daemon is restarted.
     - Rebind on rotation. Token rotates on every daemon restart; surfaces must re-read runtime.json on reconnect, never cache the token to disk.
     - Max frame size 1 MiB. Larger payloads (scrollback exports, file blobs) use a separate chunked transfer, never a single frame.

     4.3 Message envelope

     Every frame, both directions:

     {
       "v": 1,                      // protocol version, integer, required
       "id": "01J...",              // ULID, unique per message, required
       "ts": "2026-08-12T09:14:03.221Z", // ISO-8601 UTC with milliseconds, required
       "type": "evt.agent.state",   // namespaced type, required (see §4.4/§4.5)
       "corr": "01J...",            // correlation id: the `id` this responds to. null for unsolicited
       "payload": { }               // type-specific object, required (may be empty {})
     }

     Type namespaces — the mechanism that keeps the two surfaces from colliding:

     ┌────────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
     │ Prefix │    Direction     │                             Meaning                              │
     ├────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.*  │ surface → daemon │ An instruction. Always answered by res.* or err.* with corr set. │
     ├────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ res.*  │ daemon → surface │ Success response to a cmd.*.                                     │
     ├────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ err.*  │ daemon → surface │ Failure response. Payload: { code, message, retryable }.         │
     ├────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.*  │ daemon → surface │ Unsolicited event, delivered to every subscriber.                │
     └────────┴──────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

     Sub-namespaces: agent.*, job.*, transcript.*, companion.*, permission.*, audit.*, daemon.* are shared — both surfaces implement them. pty.*, fs.*, window.* are Console-only. voice.*, scene.* are Orb-only.

     Forward-compatibility rule (mandatory): an unknown type, or an unknown field inside a known payload, must be ignored silently — never an error, never a crash. This is what lets one surface ship a new feature without breaking the other.

     4.4 Events — daemon → surface

     Shared. Both surfaces subscribe to all of these.

     ┌─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
     │          Type           │                  Payload                  │                                          Notes                                          │
     ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.agent.state         │ { companionId, state, detail? }           │ state ∈ idle | listening | thinking | speaking | working. The Orb drives the sphere     │
     │                         │                                           │ from this; the Console drives its status bar from the same event.                       │
     ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.companion.status    │ { companionId, name, state, busy,         │ Roster changes and per-companion status.                                                │
     │                         │ tools[], scope }                          │                                                                                         │
     ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

     │ evt.permission.request  │ { requestId, tier, tool, args,            │ tier ∈ green | amber | red. provenance is required — see §7.3. Both surfaces can render │
     │                         │ provenance, expiresAt }                   │  the approval card.                                                                     │
     ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.permission.resolved │ { requestId, decision, decidedBy,         │ Broadcast so the other surface dismisses its card.                                      │
     │                         │ remembered }                              │                                                                                         │
     ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.audit.appended      │ { entryId, actor, tool, tier, summary }   │ For the live audit viewer.                                                              │
     ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.daemon.health       │ { uptimeS, cpuPct, memMB, apiReachable,   │ Heartbeat, every 5 s.                                                                   │
     │                         │ budgetSpent, budgetCap }                  │                                                                                         │
     ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.daemon.shutdown     │ { reason, restarting }                    │ Surfaces show a reconnect state.                                                        │
     ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.notification        │ { level, title, body, actions[] }         │ level ∈ info | warn | error.                                                            │
     └─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

     Console-only events

     ┌─────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

     ├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ evt.pty.data            │ { sessionId, seq, dataB64 } — raw PTY bytes, base64. Ordered by seq.                 │
