     Orb-only events (listed so Console never claims these names): evt.voice.wake, evt.voice.vad, evt.voice.partialTranscript, evt.voice.amplitude, evt.scene.state.

     4.5 Commands — surface → daemon

     Shared

     ┌─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
     │              Type               │                            Payload                             │             Response             │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.hello                       │ { token, surface, surfaceVersion, protocolVersion }            │ res.hello                        │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.subscribe                   │ { topics[] } — glob-ish prefixes, e.g. ["agent.*","job.*"]     │ res.subscribe                    │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.agent.message               │ { companionId, text, attachments[]? }                          │ res.agent.accepted { messageId } │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.agent.cancel                │ { companionId, messageId? }                                    │ res.ok                           │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.companion.switch            │ { companionId }                                                │ res.ok                           │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.job.create                  │ { kind, title, args, tier }                                    │ res.job.created { jobId }        │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.job.cancel / cmd.job.retry  │ { jobId }                                                      │ res.ok                           │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.permission.respond          │ { requestId, decision, remember? } — decision ∈ approve | deny │ res.ok                           │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.config.get / cmd.config.set │ { key, value? }                                                │ res.config                       │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.audit.query                 │ { since?, limit, filter? }                                     │ res.audit { entries[] }          │
     ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.ping                        │ {}                                                             │ res.pong                         │
     └─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

     Console-only

     ┌───────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┐
     │             Type              │                   Payload                   │               Response               │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.pty.spawn                 │ { profileId, cwd?, cols, rows, env? }       │ res.pty.spawned { sessionId }        │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.pty.write                 │ { sessionId, dataB64 }                      │ none (fire-and-forget)               │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.pty.resize                │ { sessionId, cols, rows }                   │ res.ok                               │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.pty.kill                  │ { sessionId, signal? }                      │ res.ok                               │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.pty.list                  │ {}                                          │ res.pty.list { sessions[] }          │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.fs.list                   │ { path, includeHidden? }                    │ res.fs.accepted then evt.fs.children │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.fs.watch / cmd.fs.unwatch │ { path }                                    │ res.ok                               │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.fs.reveal                 │ { path }                                    │ res.ok — open in Windows Explorer    │
     ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ cmd.window.spawnAt            │ { path, mode } — mode ∈ window | tab | pane │ res.ok                               │
     └───────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┘

     Orb-only (reserved): cmd.voice.mute, cmd.voice.pushToTalk, cmd.voice.setVoice, cmd.scene.setMode.

     4.6 Design tokens — shared, packages/tokens

     Source of truth is packages/tokens/tokens.json; CSS custom properties and a Python constants module are generated from it. Neither surface hard-codes a hex value.

     /* Colour — from ZOEY_OS-spec.md §3.8, authoritative */
     --bg-void:       #08080A;   --bg-ambient:    #0D1524;
     --panel:         rgba(18,18,22,0.72);
     --panel-border:  rgba(255,255,255,0.06);
     --accent:        #FF6B1A;   --accent-dim:    #B84D12;
     --sphere-hot:    #FF3B00;   --sphere-cool:   #FFA94D;
     --status-active: #22C55E;
     --text:          #E8E8EA;   --text-muted:    #6B6B72;

     /* Added for Console — semantic, not new brand colours */
     --status-warn:   #FFA94D;   /* reuses sphere-cool */
     --status-error:  #FF3B00;   /* reuses sphere-hot */
     --status-idle:   #6B6B72;
     --prov-human:    transparent;        /* provenance gutter, §7.3 */
     --prov-program:  rgba(107,107,114,0.55);
     --prov-agent:    rgba(255,107,26,0.75);

     /* Type scale — 1.250 major third, base 13px */
     --font-mono: "JetBrains Mono", ui-monospace, "Cascadia Mono", Consolas, monospace;
     --fs-label: 10px;  --fs-sm: 11px;  --fs-base: 13px;
     --fs-md: 16px;     --fs-lg: 20px;  --fs-xl: 25px;
     --lh-tight: 1.2;   --lh-base: 1.5;
     --label-transform: uppercase;  --label-tracking: 0.14em;

     /* Spacing — 4px base */
     --sp-1: 4px;  --sp-2: 8px;  --sp-3: 12px; --sp-4: 16px;
     --sp-5: 24px; --sp-6: 32px; --sp-7: 48px;

     /* Radii + layout */
     --panel-radius: 12px; --radius-sm: 6px; --radius-pill: 999px;
     --rail-w: 48px; --panel-left-w: 240px; --panel-right-w: 280px; --transcript-w: 320px;

     Standing style rules (both surfaces): no emoji · no blue-purple gradients · no icon soup · labels are uppercase mono, 10px, 0.14em tracking · centre stage floats over void with no card background.

     ▎ ⚠️ At 1366×768, rail 48 + left 240 + right 280 + transcript 320 = 888px of chrome leaves only ~478px for the terminal. The Console must default to rail + terminal + one collapsible side panel, with the full four-panel layout available only above ~1600px. Flagged to the Orb session too.

     4.7 Versioning rule

     1. PROTOCOL_VERSION is a single integer at the top of this file. It appears in every envelope as v and in the handshake.
     2. Additive changes — a new evt.*/cmd.* type, or a new optional payload field — do not bump the version. They are legal because §4.3 mandates that unknown types and fields are ignored. They must be appended to the tables above and to the changelog.
     3. Breaking changes — renaming/removing a type or field, changing a field's type, changing enum semantics, or altering the handshake — bump PROTOCOL_VERSION, and require: owner approval, both surface sessions updating together, and the daemon rejecting mismatched clients with close code 4409.
     4. Neither surface session edits this file. A change is proposed to the owner as a diff with rationale; the owner applies it. This is the whole point of the contract.
     5. The daemon is the referee: if a surface sends a type not in this document, the daemon replies err.protocol.unknownType and audit-logs it.
     6. Enum values are closed sets. Adding a value to state, status, or tier is a breaking change (consumers switch on them exhaustively).

     — end CONTRACT.md —

     ---
     5. Repository layout ⚪

     C:\dev\zoey\                       # NOT in OneDrive — deliberate
     ├── CONTRACT.md                    # §4. Read every session. Never edited by a surface session.
     ├── package.json                   # npm workspaces root
     ├── packages\
     │   ├── protocol\                  # SHARED — ask before editing
     │   │   ├── schema\*.json          #   JSON Schema = source of truth
     │   │   ├── src\index.ts           #   generated TS types + envelope helpers + validator
     │   │   └── gen\python\            #   generated TypedDicts for core/
     │   └── tokens\                    # SHARED — ask before editing
     │       ├── tokens.json            #   source of truth
     │       ├── dist\tokens.css        #   generated
     │       └── dist\tokens.py         #   generated
     ├── core\                          # SHARED — Python daemon (ZOEY_OS core)
     │   ├── server.py                  #   WS server, auth handshake (§4.2)
     │   ├── security\{guard,audit,secrets}.py
     │   ├── pty\supervisor.py          #   owns PTY sessions
     │   ├── fs\{enumerate,watch}.py    #   metadata-only tree (§7.2)
     │   └── config\permissions.yaml    #   green/amber/red — already specified in ZOEY_OS
     ├── apps\
     │   ├── console\                   # ← THIS SESSION OWNS THIS
     │   │   ├── src\main\              #   Electron main: WS client, token read, window mgmt
     │   │   ├── src\preload\           #   contextBridge, no nodeIntegration
     │   │   └── src\renderer\          #   React: terminal, tree, tabs
     │   └── orb\                       # ← OTHER SESSION OWNS THIS. NEVER EDIT.
     └── docs\ZOEY_OS-spec.md           # copied from OneDrive, becomes the canonical copy

     Ownership rule: this session touches apps/console and, with your approval, core/pty and core/fs. It never touches apps/orb. Changes to packages/* or CONTRACT.md are proposed to you, not made.

     ---
     6. Phase 1 architecture

     Scope, per your correction: tabs + ConPTY + xterm + lazy file tree + CONTRACT.md. Nothing else.

     6.1 The PTY decision ⚪ (resolving a fleet conflict)

     🔵 The fleet contradicted itself: the PTY-core agent proposed writing a standalone Go ConPTY host and explicitly rejected node-pty; the shell-profiles agent proposed @lydell/node-pty and explicitly rejected the Go host.

     Decision: @lydell/node-pty 1.2.0-beta.15. The Go host is rejected for Phase 1.

     The PTY-core agent's stated reason for rejecting node-pty was that it is "NAN-based, ABI-pinned" and would "likely refuse to load on Node v25 (modules=141)." 🟢 That is incorrect for node-pty 1.x — I checked the registry directly: node-pty 1.1.0 depends on node-addon-api@^7.1.0, i.e. it is a Node-API addon. N-API is ABI-stable across Node and Electron versions, which is precisely why no rebuild is needed. The rejection rested on a false premise, so it does not stand.

     Weighed against that: a hand-rolled Go ConPTY host means hand-packing COORD, PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_PSEUDOCONSOLE (0x00020016), and attribute-list sizing with no compiler validation — the agent itself named this as "where handle leaks hide" — plus a third language on a 2-core machine and a solo part-time schedule. Not Phase 1 work.

     🔵 Worth keeping from the PTY agent regardless:
     - Real ConPTY defects on 23H2: ClosePseudoConsole hang, lingering conhost, no DCS passthrough, reflow damage. Build 22631 predates several fixes.
     - Mitigation, adopted: bundle the Microsoft.Windows.Console.ConPTY redistributable (conpty.dll + OpenConsole.exe) and enable node-pty's useConptyDll: true, getting a newer ConPTY than the OS ships. Cost ~12 MB, and OpenConsole.exe is on the LOLBAS list so it needs an AV-allowlist note.
     - Process-tree teardown via Job Objects + JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, so a killed tab cannot orphan a dev server on port 3000.
     - Environment correctness: build the child environment from CreateEnvironmentBlock + a fresh read of HKCU\Environment, so npm install -g and pip install --user land where you expect. Assert at startup that the daemon is not running as LocalSystem — Session 0 isolation silently redirects APPDATA and breaks every global install.

     6.2 Terminal rendering 🔵→⚪

     @xterm/xterm 6.0.0 + @xterm/addon-fit + @xterm/addon-webgl, with a pre-flight GPU probe before term.open() and a DOM-renderer fallback. 🟢 On HD 620 with a legacy driver this is not optional; xterm 6 removed the canvas addon, so DOM is the only fallback rung. Benchmark the DOM path in week 1.

     Phase 1 keeps it deliberately small on a 2-core CPU:
     - Scrollback capped at 8,000 lines (~16 MB), not 100k (~195 MB).
     - No Web Worker — the fleet's off-thread decimation would contend with Electron's existing processes on 2 physical cores. Revisit only with a benchmark.
     - Bundle JetBrains Mono as woff2 — 🔵 it is not installed on this machine, and the ligature library chain (font-finder, font-ligatures) has been unmaintained since 2022. Ligatures off in Phase 1.
     - 🔵 xterm 6.0.0 is a breaking release (canvas addon gone, windowsMode and fastScrollModifier removed, viewport rewritten) and most online guidance still targets v5. Pin exact versions, not caret ranges.

     6.3 Process & window model ⚪

     ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
     │ Electron MAIN process  (Node)                          │
     │  • reads %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json (token,port) │
     │  • owns the ONLY WebSocket client → 127.0.0.1:47600    │
     │  • sets Origin: zoey://console  (renderers cannot)     │
     │  • window manager: spawnAt(path) → new BrowserWindow   │
     └───────────────▲────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     │ contextBridge IPC (no nodeIntegration,
                     │ contextIsolation ON, sandbox ON)
     ┌───────────────▼────────────────────────────────────────┐
     │ RENDERER (React)  — one per window                     │
     │  • xterm.js grid · file tree · tab bar                 │
     │  • NEVER sees the token, never opens a socket          │
     └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     │  (Phase 1: PTY in main process)
     ┌───────────────▼────────────────────────────────────────┐
     │ Python daemon (core/) — WS server, permission guard,   │
     │ audit log, fs enumeration.  Also serves apps\orb.      │
     └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

     Why the WS client is in main, not the renderer: a renderer is a browser context. It cannot set an arbitrary Origin, and giving it the auth token puts the token one XSS away from any content rendered in the window. Main-process-only means §4.2's Origin allowlist is enforceable and the token never enters a web context.

     Click a folder → new console window, end to end:
     1. Renderer: click on tree node → window.zoey.spawnAt(path, mode) over contextBridge.
     2. Main: validates path is a real directory (resolve, reject reparse-point traversal outside allowed roots), then either creates a new BrowserWindow or adds a tab.
     3. New renderer boots, requests cmd.pty.spawn { profileId: default, cwd: path, cols, rows }.
     4. Daemon's permission guard checks the path against protected-path policy, spawns via node-pty, returns sessionId.
     5. evt.pty.data streams back → xterm. Shift-click = new window, Ctrl-click = tab, plain click = whichever you set as default.

     6.4 Shell profiles — how "all of CMD, PowerShell, WSL" is honestly satisfied 🔵

     We host the real binaries under ConPTY. We reimplement nothing. No built-in cd, no emulated dir/ls. Every feature of cmd.exe, PowerShell 5.1, bash, and every WSL distro is inherited because it is genuinely that program running. Reimplementing even PowerShell's parser would be years of work for a strictly worse result.

     🔵 Discovery, verified on your machine:
     - WSL distros from HKCU\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Lxss (fast path; confirmed Ubuntu-22.04, docker-desktop), falling back to wsl.exe -l -q with WSL_UTF8=1 injected — without it the output is UTF-16 and parses as garbage (this is exactly the mojibake seen in my own first probe).
     - Git Bash from HKLM\SOFTWARE\GitForWindows.
     - Docker via \\.\pipe\dockerDesktopLinuxEngine — 🟢 currently absent because Docker Desktop isn't running, so the provider must degrade to "engine offline", not throw.
     - Profiles in profiles.jsonc (comments allowed), validated with zod, carrying a schemaVersion from day one.

     What must still be built because hosting doesn't provide it: tabs/panes, the file tree, block semantics (later), the chat layer (later), the security guard, and cross-shell history.

     ---
     7. Security architecture

     7.1 Phase 1 non-negotiables (cannot be retrofitted)

     1. WS auth — loopback bind + Origin allowlist + per-launch token + 3 s handshake deadline (§4.2). Without this, any webpage commands your agent.
     2. Electron hardening — contextIsolation: true, nodeIntegration: false, sandbox: true, no remote module. Token stays in main.
     3. Audit log from commit one — append-only, hash-chained, every command with actor ∈ {user, agent, schedule}. Retrofitting provenance later is impossible because the information is gone.
     4. Protected paths — C:\dev\zoey, C:\Users\SERIOUS-PC\OneDrive, and system dirs marked protected in permissions.yaml. Writes/deletes there confirm regardless of origin.
     5. Secret redaction before any write — history, logs, session records. A secret written unredacted once is leaked permanently.

     7.2 Filesystem indexing — your correction #4 ⚪

     - Metadata only. Name, size, mtime, attributes, reparse tag. Never open a file to read content during indexing.
     - Never read content from a reparse point. 🟢 17,340 exist in your tree; reading a dehydrated placeholder triggers a download on metered data with 14.5 GB free.
     - OneDrive is excluded from content indexing by default, opt-in per folder, never recursive-by-default.
     - 🔵 Hydration Firewall (adopted from the fleet, and a genuinely good idea): compute bytes-to-download as EndOfFile − AllocationSize from attributes alone. Show it per row and aggregate per folder — "expanding this costs 3.4 GB". Register fs.hydrate as an amber rule in the existing permissions.yaml so a grep -r, a build, or Claude Code reading files gets intercepted with an exact figure before it spends your data. Emitted as evt.fs.hydrationWarning (§4.4).
     - Phase 1 does not ship the SQLite snapshot or the USN journal reader — those were fleet proposals for later phases. Phase 1 is lazy enumeration on expand, virtualized rows, node_modules/.git/dist/venv collapsed by default.
     - 🔵 Honest limitation to surface in the UI: live file-watching inside WSL2 is impossible from Windows — ReadDirectoryChangesW returns ERROR_INVALID_FUNCTION over 9P (microsoft/WSL#7674). WSL paths get manual refresh, and the UI says so rather than pretending.

     7.3 Terminal output is untrusted — your correction #5 ⚪

     Phase 1 has no AI chat, but the data model must be right now or it cannot be fixed later.

     - Every byte captured from a PTY is stored with a provenance tag: human (you typed it), program (process stdout/stderr), or agent (model-proposed). Tags live in the daemon's session store and never travel inside the PTY byte stream.
     - When the AI layer lands, program output is passed to the model inside explicit delimiters, labelled as data, with a standing system rule that content inside them is never an instruction.
     - A red-tier action can never be triggered while untrusted content is in context without your explicit approval — this is the existing ZOEY_OS rule, and it is enforced by the daemon's guard, not by prompt wording.
     - The model never receives a raw command string to execute. It selects a tool name + structured args; Python owns execution. Straight from ZOEY_OS-spec §6.
     - 🔵 Trust Paint (adopted, deferred to the phase that adds AI): render provenance as a subtle left gutter band — human, program, and agent text look different at a glance. Because tags arrive over the WebSocket keyed to (sessionId, byteOffset) and any provenance-shaped sequence arriving from the PTY is stripped before parsing, a hostile npm postinstall printing a fake "agent approved" banner cannot paint itself as trusted. Tokens --prov-human/-program/-agent are already reserved in §4.6.

     7.4 Honest residual risk ⚪

     This tool's purpose is executing arbitrary commands. Anything you type runs. Real protection is: the AI cannot act unreviewed, secrets don't leak into logs or model context, destructive operations on protected paths require confirmation, and everything is auditable. It is not a sandbox and must not be described as one. True isolation would mean running commands inside WSL2/Docker by default, which breaks normal Windows dev work — available as an opt-in tier later, not the default.

     ---

     9. Deferred design notes (so Phase 1 doesn't block them)

     9.1 Shell integration for blocks — Phase 2 🟢 OSC 133 marks (A prompt, B command start, C output start, D;<exit> end). Verified traps:
     - PowerShell 5.1: Microsoft's official sample uses the `e escape, which **does not exist before PowerShell 6**. You have 5.1 only. Must use $([char]27) or the prompt emits literal garbage.
     - cmd.exe: Microsoft states plainly it cannot read the previous command's exit code in the prompt — cmd blocks get boundaries but no pass/fail colouring. Accept it.
     - bash: full support; needs PS0 (bash ≥ 4.4; Ubuntu 22.04 ships 5.1). ✅
     - The Console injects these into a transient profile at launch — it never edits your real $PROFILE.

     9.2 Windows Explorer context menu — Phase 5 🟢 Ship a legacy registry verb only. Windows 11 demotes those to "Show more options"; the primary menu needs IExplorerCommand + app identity via a Sparse MSIX package and a C++ COM DLL — i.e. MSVC, which you don't have. Cheap to ship the legacy verb, and your real requirement is satisfied inside the app anyway.

     ---
     10. Cut list ⚪

     Rejected deliberately, so these aren't re-litigated:

     ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
     │                   Cut                    │                                                        Why                                                         │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Tauri v2                                 │ Needs Rust + MSVC ≈ 3–7 GB on metered data against 14.5 GB free.                                                   │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Custom Go ConPTY host                    │ Third language; hand-rolled syscalls with no compiler validation; premise for rejecting node-pty was factually     │
     │                                          │ wrong (§6.1).                                                                                                      │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ A custom Zoey shell language             │ 🔵 Fleet proposed it; cut. You need a console that runs your projects, not a new language to learn. Host real      │
     │                                          │ shells.                                                                                                            │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Reimplementing CMD/PowerShell/WSL        │ Infinite scope, strictly worse result. Host the real binaries.                                                     │
     │ builtins                                 │                                                                                                                    │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Primary-menu Windows 11 shell extension  │ Needs MSVC + sparse MSIX. Legacy verb instead.                                                                     │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Web Worker stream decimation (Phase 1)   │ 2 physical cores; would contend with Electron's own processes. Benchmark first.                                    │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Ligatures (Phase 1)                      │ Dependency chain unmaintained since 2022; bundle JetBrains Mono woff2 instead.                                     │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ USN journal reader / Time-Scrubber       │ Requires an elevated Windows Service + NTFS; degrades silently in your actual configuration. Later, if ever.       │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Sixel / inline images (Phase 1)          │ Gated on ConPTY DCS passthrough that 22631 lacks.                                                                  │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Four-panel layout at launch              │ 888px of chrome on a 1366px display leaves 478px of terminal.                                                      │
     ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │ Content indexing of OneDrive             │ Your correction #4. Metadata only.                                                                                 │
     └──────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

     ---
     11. Verification

     Phase 0 — the contract and the auth boundary
     - Bad token → close 4401; bad Origin (http://evil.com) → close 4401, audit entry written; no cmd.hello in 3 s → close 4408. Test all three with a raw ws script.
     - Confirm runtime.json ACL: icacls %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json shows only your SID and SYSTEM.
     - Browser drive-by test: open a page with new WebSocket('ws://127.0.0.1:47600/v1') in Chrome; it must be rejected on Origin and logged.
     - Unknown-type test: send cmd.nonexistent.thing; daemon replies err.protocol.unknownType and does not drop the connection. Send an unknown field in a known payload; it is ignored.

     Phase 1 — the console actually works
     - npm install in a scratch dir under C:\dev; npm install -g <pkg> lands in C:\Users\SERIOUS-PC\AppData\Roaming\npm\node_modules; pip install --user succeeds. These are your requirement #10 and #7 — test them explicitly.
     - claude runs interactively in a tab and renders correctly.
     - Ctrl+C interrupts a running ping -t. Closing a tab running a dev server frees port 3000 (netstat -ano | findstr :3000 is empty).
     - Every profile launches: cmd, PowerShell 5.1, Git Bash, Ubuntu-22.04, docker-desktop.
     - Tree paints in < 200 ms at the OneDrive root; expanding Udemy Course Folders stays responsive; node_modules collapsed by default; hydration cost shown for a cloud folder.
     - Click a folder → new window with that cwd; cd confirms.
     - Renderer benchmark on your actual hardware: GPU probe result recorded; if WebGL2 is unavailable, DOM renderer sustains a 50k-line dir /s without freezing the UI.
     - Power-cut test: kill the daemon mid-npm install; relaunch; session state and audit chain both intact.

     Continuous
     - packages/protocol schema validation runs in CI over every fixture message.
     - A lint rule fails the build on any hard-coded hex colour in apps/console (tokens only).

│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ ---                                                                                                                                                           │
│ 5. Build order — cheapest risk first                                                                                                                          │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Deliberately ordered to protect metered data: the make-or-break native-module question is answered for ~2 MB, before Electron's ~250 MB is touched.           │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Step 0 — Prove the PTY binary loads. Hard gate.                                                                                                               │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Install @lydell/node-pty@1.1.0 alone. From plain Node, spawn cmd.exe, write echo hello, read bytes back, confirm exit code.                                   │
│ - Pass → proceed. Fail → stop and report; the architecture changes and no Electron download is wasted.                                                        │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ ⚠️ This gate is necessary but NOT sufficient, and I want to be precise about why. node-pty 1.x builds on node-addon-api@^7, i.e. Node-API, which is designed to be ABI-stable across Node versions and across other VMs including Electron — that is the whole premise of shipping one prebuilt binary. In practice N-API modules do load in Electron without electron-rebuild.                                                                                                         │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ But I could not find an unambiguous primary-source statement confirming it: Electron's own "Native Node Modules" page does not mention N-API at all, and says only that native modules "need to be recompiled for Electron" because of its different ABI — guidance aimed at old-style node-ABI addons. So the claim rests on design intent plus common practice, not on a citation.                                                                                                     │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Therefore Step 2 re-proves it inside Electron, and that is the real gate. If it fails there, the fallback is node-pty's own prebuild download path, and failing that, hosting the PTY in a small Node sidecar outside Electron. Neither costs the Electron download twice.                                            │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Step 1 — Electron shell                                                                                                                                       │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Install Electron + electron-vite + vite@7 + React. Blank window with contextIsolation: true, nodeIntegration: false, sandbox: true.                           │
│ Measure and record: cold start, idle RAM, on this actual machine.                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Three config constraints, verified during planning 🟢 — each fails on first run if missed:                                                                    │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ 1. ws and @lydell/node-pty must be externalized. Bundling a native module breaks it, and bundling ws pulls in Node built-ins Vite will try to polyfill. Use externalizeDepsPlugin().                                                                                                                                      │
│ 2. @zoey/protocol must be excluded from that externalization — i.e. bundled. Its package.json entry is "main": "./src/index.ts": raw TypeScript with no build step and noEmit: true. Externalized, Electron's Node would require a .ts file at runtime and throw. So: externalizeDepsPlugin({ exclude: ['@zoey/protocol'] }). It also imports ./enums.generated.ts with the extension (allowImportingTsExtensions), which Vite resolves when bundling and Node would not.               │
│ 3. sandbox: true is compatible with the preload plan. Electron's docs confirm a sandboxed preload can still use contextBridge and ipcRenderer, and may only require electron, events, timers, url. Ours needs nothing else — but it means the preload can never require('ws') or touch the filesystem, which is exactly the property we want.                                                                                                                                         │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Step 2 — PTY in a utilityProcess                                                                                                                              │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ src/pty-host/index.ts hosts @lydell/node-pty. Main spawns it via utilityProcess.fork() and hands the renderer a MessagePort. Bytes flow PTY → utilityProcess → MessagePort → renderer, never through main, never through Python (CONTRACT §4.2).                                                                           │
│ A native crash kills one tab's backend, not the window.                                                                                                       │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Step 3 — xterm + renderer ladder                                                                                                                              │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ @xterm/xterm + addon-fit. Pre-flight GPU probe before term.open(); attach addon-webgl only if it passes, else DOM.                                            │
│ Measure keystroke → glyph against the spec §4 budget: p95 16 ms, hard fail 50 ms. Record the real number, including on the DOM path.                          │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Step 4 — Wire the daemon (this is what makes it legal)                                                                                                        │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ - src/main/token.ts — read %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json; ignore a stale file whose pid is dead; never cache the token to disk.                            │
│ - src/main/daemon.ts — if no live daemon, spawn python core/server.py, wait for the handshake, supervise it.                                                  │
│ - src/main/ws-client.ts — ws with origin: 'zoey://console', cmd.hello inside 3 s, reconnect re-reads runtime.json because the token rotates every launch.     │
│ - cmd.pty.requestSpawn → res.pty.grant before a single PTY is created. Handle err.permission.pending (approval card) and err.permission.denied.               │
│ - cmd.pty.report on started / exited / titleChanged / cwdChanged / startFailed — the last reclaims a dangling grant, which is exactly why it was added to the enum.                                                                                                                                                         │
│ - Honour evt.pty.revoke.                                                                                                                                      │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Step 5 — Verify the exit criterion                                                                                                                            │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ See §7.                                                                                                                                                       │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ ---                                                                                                                                                           │
│ 6. Files created (all under apps/console/, per STRUCTURE v2.2)                                                                                                │

│ apps\console\                                                                                                                                                 │

│ 7. Verification                                                                                                                                               │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Exit criterion — run each in the Console's own terminal:                                                                                                      │
│ - npm install in a scratch dir under C:\dev                                                                                                                   │
│ - npm install -g <small pkg> → lands in C:\Users\SERIOUS-PC\AppData\Roaming\npm\node_modules (this is the Session 0 / APPDATA trap; assert the daemon is not LocalSystem)                                                                                                                                                  │
│ - pip install --user <small pkg>                                                                                                                              │
│ - git status / git log in C:\dev\zoey                                                                                                                         │
│ - claude — full TUI: alt-screen, resize, colour, Ctrl+C. The hardest of the five.                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Security — must not regress Phase 0:                                                                                                                          │
│ - Renderer cannot reach the token or open a socket (check via devtools console).                                                                              │
│ - No PTY without a grant: stub the daemon to deny, confirm no terminal appears.                                                                               │
│ - Kill the PTY host mid-session → startFailed/exited reported, grant reclaimed.                                                                               │
│ - python core/test_auth.py still 22/22 with the Console connected.                                                                                            │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Performance — record real numbers, don't assume:                                                                                                              │
│ - Cold start, idle RAM, GPU probe result (webgl or dom).                                                                                                      │
│ - Keystroke → glyph p95 vs the 16 ms / 50 ms budget.                                                                                                          │
│ - dir /s in a large tree without freezing the UI.                                                                                                             │
│ - Ctrl+C interrupts ping -t; closing the window frees the process tree.                                                                                       │
│                                                                                                                                                               │
│ Continuous: npm test (71 tests) stays green; npm run contract:check passes.                                                                                   │

The externalizeDeps config for the Orb session

Verified from the installed types, not docs: node_modules/electron-vite/dist/index.d.ts marks externalizeDepsPlugin @deprecated use 'build.externalizeDeps' config option instead. The type is build.externalizeDeps?: boolean | { exclude?: string[]; include?: string[] }.

main: {
  build: {
    externalizeDeps: { exclude: ['@zoey/protocol', '@zoey/tokens'] },
    rollupOptions: {
      external: ['@lydell/node-pty'],          // belt-and-braces on the addon
      input: { index: resolve(__dirname, 'src/main/index.ts') },
      // Step 2 adds 'pty-host' as a SECOND INPUT HERE — not a new config
      // section — so it inherits main's externalization and node-pty stays external.
    },
  },
},
preload: { build: { externalizeDeps: { exclude: ['@zoey/protocol'] } } },
renderer: { root: resolve(__dirname, 'src/renderer'), plugins: [react()] },

The rule underneath it: dependency class is the switch. electron-vite externalizes dependencies, bundles devDependencies. @lydell/node-pty and ws must be dependencies; @zoey/protocol and @zoey/tokens must be devDependencies because protocol's entry is raw .ts. The exclude list is belt-and-braces so nobody later "tidies" them into dependencies and breaks launch. The Orb reached the same conclusion independently — only the API name in its comment is stale.

Step 1 — hardened blank window: works

Build clean across all three configs. Cold start 425–493 ms, main RSS ~76–107 MB (~687 MB total across 8 processes — Electron's normal process fan-out, worth watching against your free RAM).

Both security assertions verified at runtime, not assumed:
[renderer:info] SELFCHECK nodeAccess=none (sandboxed)
[renderer:info] SELFCHECK contextBridge=ok

The install cost almost nothing. Only 3 packages in 8 seconds — Electron, Vite, React and electron-vite were already hoisted from the Orb's install, and Electron's 225 MB binary was already on disk. The shared node_modules you warned me about saved the entire download.

Two things worth flagging

        rows: dims?.rows ?? 24,

      return { ok: false as const, error: (err as Error).message }

  })

import { contextBridge, ipcRenderer } from 'electron'
import { PTY_PORT_CHANNEL, type PtyHostKind } from '../shared/pty-ipc.ts'

/** Surface exposed to the renderer as `window.zoey`. */
export interface PtyStartOk {
  ok: true
  kind: PtyHostKind
  workerOk: boolean
  probeMs: number
  probeError?: string
  pid?: number
}
export interface PtyStartErr {
  ok: false
  error: string
}

const api = {
  /** Round-trip liveness check. Step 1 only — proves the bridge works. */
  /** Round-trip liveness check. Proves the bridge works under sandbox:true. */
  ping: (): Promise<string> => ipcRenderer.invoke('zoey:ping'),

  /** Build/runtime facts the renderer is allowed to know. */
  /**
   * Ask main for a terminal.
   *
   * Note what is NOT here: the renderer cannot name a shell, a cwd, or an
   * argv. It passes dimensions and nothing else. Main chooses the shell and —
   * from Step 4 — must obtain a daemon grant first. Letting the renderer supply
   * a command line would make this bridge an unguarded execution channel, which
   * is the exact hole the red-team flagged.
   */
  startPty: (dims: { cols: number; rows: number }): Promise<PtyStartOk | PtyStartErr> =>
    ipcRenderer.invoke('zoey:pty-start', dims),

  /**
   * Receive the MessagePort carrying terminal bytes.
   *
   * The port is transferred, not copied, and never passes through the main
   * process again — main is out of the data path once this fires.
   */
  onPtyPort: (cb: (port: MessagePort) => void): void => {
    ipcRenderer.on(PTY_PORT_CHANNEL, (event) => {
      const port = event.ports[0]
      if (port) cb(port)
    })
  },

  versions: {
    electron: process.versions.electron,
    chrome: process.versions.chrome,

if (process.contextIsolated) {
  contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('zoey', api)
} else {
  // Should be unreachable: contextIsolation is set true in webPreferences.
  // Failing loudly beats silently degrading the security model.
  // Unreachable: contextIsolation is true in webPreferences. Failing loudly
  // beats silently degrading the security model.
  throw new Error('contextIsolation is disabled — refusing to expose the Zoey API')
}

type Bridge = 'checking' | 'ok' | 'failed'

/**
 * Step 1 — a blank, hardened window that proves four things and nothing more:
 *   1. Electron + electron-vite + React render at all
 *   2. the contextBridge round-trips (so the preload loaded under sandbox:true)
 *   3. the renderer has NO Node access — verified, not assumed
 *   4. design tokens resolve from packages/tokens (no hex lives in this file)
 * Step 2 — prove the PTY reaches the renderer over a MessagePort.
 *
 * The terminal arrives in Step 3. Deliberately not here.
 * Deliberately NOT xterm. That is Step 3. This renders raw bytes into a <pre>
 * so that if something is wrong, it is wrong in the transport and not hidden
 * behind a terminal emulator's own parsing.
 */
export default function App(): React.JSX.Element {
  const [bridge, setBridge] = useState<'checking' | 'ok' | 'failed'>('checking')
  const [bridge, setBridge] = useState<Bridge>('checking')
  const [host, setHost] = useState<string>('not started')
  const [probe, setProbe] = useState<string>('—')
  const [out, setOut] = useState<string>('')
  const portRef = useRef<MessagePort | null>(null)
  const preRef = useRef<HTMLPreElement | null>(null)

  const nodeLeaks = (['require', 'process', 'module', 'global', 'Buffer'] as const).filter(
    (k) => k in globalThis,
  )

  useEffect(() => {
    window.zoey
      ?.ping()
      .then((r) => {
        const state = r === 'pong' ? 'ok' : 'failed'
        setBridge(state)
        console.log(`SELFCHECK contextBridge=${state}`)
        const s: Bridge = r === 'pong' ? 'ok' : 'failed'
        setBridge(s)
        console.log(`SELFCHECK contextBridge=${s}`)
      })
      .catch((e) => {
        setBridge('failed')
        console.log(`SELFCHECK contextBridge=failed (${String(e)})`)
      })
  }, [])
      .catch(() => setBridge('failed'))

  // CONTRACT §7.1 / plan §7: the renderer must have no Node reach. If any of
  // these are defined, contextIsolation or sandbox has regressed and the whole
  // security model is void — so it is surfaced in the UI, not buried in a test.
  const nodeLeaks = (
    ['require', 'process', 'module', 'global', 'Buffer'] as const
  ).filter((k) => k in globalThis)

  useEffect(() => {
    console.log(
      nodeLeaks.length === 0
        ? 'SELFCHECK nodeAccess=none (sandboxed)'
        : `SELFCHECK nodeAccess=LEAKED ${nodeLeaks.join(',')}`,
    )
  }, [nodeLeaks])

  const v = window.zoey?.versions
    // Main transfers the port once the host has spawned.
    window.zoey?.onPtyPort((port) => {
      portRef.current = port
      port.onmessage = (ev: MessageEvent) => {
        const m = ev.data as PtyFromHost
        switch (m.t) {
          case 'ready':
            console.log(`SELFCHECK ptyReady pid=${m.pid}`)
            break
          case 'data':
            setOut((prev) => (prev + atob(m.b64)).slice(-4000))
            break
          case 'exit':
            setOut((prev) => `${prev}\n[exited code=${m.code}]`)
            console.log(`SELFCHECK ptyExit code=${m.code}`)
            break
          case 'error':
            setOut((prev) => `${prev}\n[error ${m.message}]`)
            break
        }
      }
      port.start()
      console.log('SELFCHECK ptyPort=received')
    })
  }, [])

  useEffect(() => {
    if (preRef.current) preRef.current.scrollTop = preRef.current.scrollHeight
  }, [out])

  const send = (m: PtyToHost): void => portRef.current?.postMessage(m)

  const start = useCallback(async () => {
    setHost('starting…')
    const r = await window.zoey.startPty({ cols: 100, rows: 24 })
    if (r.ok) {
      setHost(`${r.kind}${r.pid ? ` pid=${r.pid}` : ''}`)
      setProbe(`worker_threads PASSED (${r.probeMs.toFixed(0)} ms)`)
      console.log(`SELFCHECK ptyHost=${r.kind} workerOk=${r.workerOk}`)
    } else {
      setHost('FAILED')
      setProbe(r.error)
      console.log(`SELFCHECK ptyHost=failed ${r.error}`)
    }
  }, [])

  const runEcho = (): void =>
    send({ t: 'write', b64: btoa('echo ZOEY_STEP2_OK\r') })

  return (
    <main className="shell">
      <h1 className="brand">ZOEY CONSOLE</h1>
      <p className="phase">PHASE 1A · STEP 1</p>
      <p className="phase">PHASE 1A · STEP 2 — PTY OVER MESSAGEPORT</p>

      <dl className="facts">
        <dt>contextBridge</dt>

          {nodeLeaks.length === 0 ? 'none — sandboxed' : `LEAKED: ${nodeLeaks.join(', ')}`}
        </dd>

        <dt>electron</dt>
        <dd>{v?.electron ?? '—'}</dd>
        <dt>pty host</dt>
        <dd data-state={host.startsWith('utilityProcess') ? 'ok' : host === 'FAILED' ? 'failed' : 'checking'}>
          {host}
        </dd>

        <dt>chromium</dt>
        <dd>{v?.chrome ?? '—'}</dd>

        <dt>node</dt>
        <dd>{v?.node ?? '—'}</dd>
        <dt>worker probe</dt>
        <dd>{probe}</dd>
      </dl>

      <p className="next">Next: utilityProcess Worker probe, then xterm.</p>
      <div className="row">
        <button onClick={start}>start pty</button>
        <button onClick={runEcho}>echo</button>
        <button onClick={() => send({ t: 'resize', cols: 120, rows: 40 })}>resize</button>
        <button onClick={() => send({ t: 'kill' })}>kill</button>
      </div>

      <pre className="out" ref={preRef}>
        {out || '(no output yet — press “start pty”)'}
      </pre>
    </main>
  )
}

export type ZoeyApi = typeof api

/**
 * Hand the MessagePort to the renderer's main world.
 *
 * This deliberately does NOT go through contextBridge. A MessagePort cannot
 * cross the isolated-world boundary as a bridged value — it arrives as an inert
 * proxy whose `start` and `postMessage` are not functions, which is precisely
 * what happened the first time this was wired. The supported route is
 * `window.postMessage` with a transfer list, which moves the real port object.
 *
 * Security note: this widens nothing. The port is minted by main and carries
 * only the PTY protocol in src/shared/pty-ipc.ts. The renderer still cannot
 * name a shell, a cwd or an argv — it can only write bytes to a session main
 * already authorised.
 */
ipcRenderer.on(PTY_PORT_CHANNEL, (event) => {
  window.postMessage(PTY_PORT_CHANNEL, '*', event.ports)
})

⚠️ CURRENT STATE — read this first

Steps 0 and 1 are complete and verified. Step 2 is ~90% done and apps/console/src/renderer/App.tsx is BROKEN on disk right now.

The headline result: the red-team's blocker is resolved, favourably

[pty-host] worker_threads probe PASSED in utilityProcess (39 ms)
[renderer:info] SELFCHECK ptyHost=utilityProcess workerOk=true

Stock Electron 43's utilityProcess CAN host worker_threads. node-pty's Windows conout Worker is therefore safe there, and rung 1 holds — no drop to the main-process or ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE fallbacks. The ladder stays documented in src/shared/pty-ipc.ts as insurance, unused.

Externalization verified in the built output:
- out/main/pty-host.js contains require("@lydell/node-pty") → external, correct
- out/main/index.js contains 0 runtime requires of @zoey/tokens → bundled, correct

The bug I hit, and the real cause

port.start is not a function / postMessage is not a function in the renderer.

Cause: a MessagePort cannot cross the contextIsolation boundary via contextBridge. It arrives as an inert proxy. The supported route is window.postMessage(channel, '*', event.ports) from the preload, which transfers the real port object into the main world.

Already fixed in src/preload/index.ts (the onPtyPort bridge method was removed and replaced with a window.postMessage forward).

Exactly what remains — 3 edits to apps/console/src/renderer/App.tsx

1. Add the import: import { PTY_PORT_CHANNEL } from '../shared/pty-ipc.ts'
2. Close the handler: line ~68 still reads }) from the old callback form. It must become } — onWindowMessage is a plain function now, not a callback argument.
3. Register + clean up, inside the same useEffect:
window.addEventListener('message', onWindowMessage)
return () => window.removeEventListener('message', onWindowMessage)

Then: npm run -w @zoey/console typecheck && build, relaunch, and confirm the full self-check set — ptyPort=received, ptyReady pid=…, ptyEcho=ok.

Nothing else is outstanding. Steps 3–5 (xterm + GPU probe, daemon wiring, exit criterion) are unstarted and unchanged below.

---
1. Context

Phase 0 is done and verified: the monorepo, the frozen protocol, generated tokens and enums, and a Python daemon whose auth boundary is proven by 71 passing tests (28 protocol · 21 anti-drift · 22 live auth) plus a freshness check that fails the build on stale generated output.

Phase 1a builds the first surface: an Electron window with one working terminal. Nothing else — no tabs, no file tree, no chat, no blocks.

Exit criterion (yours): npm install, pip install, git, and claude all run correctly in that terminal.

Two things make this harder than "embed xterm":

1. No MSVC. If a native module fails to load, there is no compiler to rebuild it with. The PTY layer must be prebuilt-only, and that must be proven before ~250 MB of Electron is downloaded over a metered connection.
2. CONTRACT §6.5 — no PTY may exist without a daemon grant. The terminal is not a local concern; it is authorized, audited, and revocable by Zoey Core.

⚠️ Daemon auto-start is a development stopgap

It must be labelled as such in src/main/daemon.ts and here, because the Orb session polls for the daemon rather than spawning it. If the Console owns the daemon's lifecycle, closing the Console kills the Orb's connection — a cross-surface coupling neither contract nor spec sanctions.

Rules:
- Gated on process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production' / !app.isPackaged. It must never run in a packaged build.
- If a live daemon already exists (runtime.json with a live pid), attach — never spawn a second one.
- On Console exit, only kill a daemon this process started. Never kill an inherited one.
- Phase 3 replaces this with a Windows service (spec §9). This code is expected to be deleted.

---
5. Build order — cheapest risk first

Deliberately ordered to protect metered data: the make-or-break native-module question is answered for ~2 MB, before Electron's ~250 MB is touched.

Step 0 — Smoke test, NOT the hard gate ⚠️ (corrected by the red-team)

My original framing was wrong. I called this the hard gate; it cannot be. Node-API is ABI-stable, so a plain-Node load essentially cannot fail for ABI reasons — and it cannot detect the one failure mode that actually threatens this design.

The real risk: on Windows, @lydell/node-pty constructs a worker_threads Worker for its conout connection — unconditionally, for every PTY (windowsConoutConnection.js). Electron's utilityProcess has historically thrown on new Worker() because of its V8 platform (electron#20550, #18540, #26816); the fix in #20416 covered only ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE. VS Code did move its pty host to utilityProcess (vscode#175335 → PR #182631), but VS Code ships a patched Electron — strong evidence, not proof, for stock Electron 43.

So Step 0 stays (it is ~2 MB and does prove the binary loads and the platform optional-dep resolved), but it is demoted to a smoke test. It also gains a ConPTY sanity check on this specific 22631 build: spawn cmd.exe, resize the pty, read output, and watch for hangs or assertions rather than merely "it loaded".

The hard gate moves to the first thing in Step 2: inside the Electron utilityProcess, before any node-pty code, construct a trivial worker_threads Worker. If it throws, node-pty cannot be hosted there.

Fallback ladder, pre-committed now so a failed probe is a config switch and not a rewrite:
utilityProcess → main process (worker_threads confirmed working) → child_process.fork with ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 (what VS Code used for years).
The MessagePort boundary to the renderer stays identical in all three, so the host is swappable without touching the terminal.

Step 0 (original framing, retained for the binary check)

Install @lydell/node-pty@1.1.0 alone. From plain Node, spawn cmd.exe, write echo hello, read bytes back, confirm exit code.
- Pass → proceed. Fail → stop and report; the architecture changes and no Electron download is wasted.

⚠️ This gate is necessary but NOT sufficient, and I want to be precise about why. node-pty 1.x builds on node-addon-api@^7, i.e. Node-API, which is designed to be ABI-stable across Node versions and across other VMs including Electron — that is the whole premise of shipping one prebuilt binary. In practice N-API modules do load in Electron without electron-rebuild.

But I could not find an unambiguous primary-source statement confirming it: Electron's own "Native Node Modules" page does not mention N-API at all, and says only that native modules "need to be recompiled for Electron" because of its different ABI — guidance aimed at old-style node-ABI addons. So the claim rests on design intent plus common practice, not on a citation.

Therefore Step 2 re-proves it inside Electron, and that is the real gate. If it fails there, the fallback is node-pty's own prebuild download path, and failing that, hosting the PTY in a small Node sidecar outside Electron. Neither costs the Electron download twice.

Step 1 — Electron shell

Install Electron + electron-vite + vite@7 + React. Blank window with contextIsolation: true, nodeIntegration: false, sandbox: true.
Measure and record: cold start, idle RAM, on this actual machine.

Config constraints, verified 🟢 — each fails on first run if missed:

1. Use build.externalizeDeps, NOT externalizeDepsPlugin. electron-vite's own docs: "Before electron-vite 5, you should configure it using the externalizeDepsPlugin." The plugin is the legacy API and nearly every template online still shows it. ⚠️ My earlier draft had this wrong, and apps/orb/package.json's comment references the plugin too — worth passing to the Orb session, though their mechanism is right.
2. Dependency class is the externalization switch. electron-vite externalizes dependencies and bundles devDependencies. So:
  - @lydell/node-pty and ws → dependencies (externalized, required at runtime).
  - @zoey/protocol and @zoey/tokens → devDependencies (bundled). @zoey/protocol's entry is "main": "./src/index.ts" — raw TypeScript, noEmit, importing ./enums.generated.ts with the extension. Externalized, Electron's Node would require a .ts file and crash on launch. The Orb session reached this identical conclusion independently and documented it in their package.json.
3. @lydell/node-pty goes in apps/console/package.json under dependencies, exact pin — not the root, not devDependencies. It resolves its binary through a runtime dynamic require plus an on-disk package.json read, so bundling it is fatal. Add build.rollupOptions.external: ['@lydell/node-pty'] as belt-and-braces.
4. No "type": "module" in apps/console/package.json. A sandboxed Electron preload cannot be an ES module — declaring the package ESM makes electron-vite emit ESM for main and preload, and the preload then fails to load. Source stays ESM TypeScript; electron-vite transpiles main/preload to CJS. (Credit: the Orb session found this; I did not have it.)
5. sandbox: true is compatible with the preload plan. Electron's docs confirm a sandboxed preload keeps contextBridge and ipcRenderer and may only require electron, events, timers, url. Ours needs nothing more — meaning the preload can never require('ws') or touch the filesystem, which is exactly the property we want.
6. No packager dependency in 1a. @lydell/node-pty's asar workaround only rewrites VS Code's node_modules.asar layout and is a silent no-op for a standard app.asar — which would break its conout worker. Phase 1a's exit criterion is dev-mode only, so the cheapest correct answer is to add no packager at all and settle asar: false vs asarUnpack in Phase 5.

Step 2 — PTY in a utilityProcess

src/pty-host/index.ts hosts @lydell/node-pty. Main spawns it via utilityProcess.fork() and hands the renderer a MessagePort. Bytes flow PTY → utilityProcess → MessagePort → renderer, never through main, never through Python (CONTRACT §4.2).
A native crash kills one tab's backend, not the window.

Step 3 — xterm + renderer ladder

@xterm/xterm + addon-fit. Pre-flight GPU probe before term.open(); attach addon-webgl only if it passes, else DOM.
Measure keystroke → glyph against the spec §4 budget: p95 16 ms, hard fail 50 ms. Record the real number, including on the DOM path.

Step 4 — Wire the daemon (this is what makes it legal)

- src/main/token.ts — read %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json; ignore a stale file whose pid is dead; never cache the token to disk.
- src/main/daemon.ts — if no live daemon, spawn python core/server.py, wait for the handshake, supervise it.
- src/main/ws-client.ts — ws with origin: 'zoey://console', cmd.hello inside 3 s, reconnect re-reads runtime.json because the token rotates every launch.
- cmd.pty.requestSpawn → res.pty.grant before a single PTY is created. Handle err.permission.pending (approval card) and err.permission.denied.
- cmd.pty.report on started / exited / titleChanged / cwdChanged / startFailed — the last reclaims a dangling grant, which is exactly why it was added to the enum.
- Honour evt.pty.revoke.

Step 5 — Verify the exit criterion

See §7.

7. Verification

Exit criterion — run each in the Console's own terminal:
- npm install in a scratch dir under C:\dev
- npm install -g <small pkg> → lands in C:\Users\SERIOUS-PC\AppData\Roaming\npm\node_modules (this is the Session 0 / APPDATA trap; assert the daemon is not LocalSystem)
- pip install --user <small pkg>
- git status / git log in C:\dev\zoey
- claude — full TUI: alt-screen, resize, colour, Ctrl+C. The hardest of the five.

Security — must not regress Phase 0:
- Renderer cannot reach the token or open a socket (check via devtools console).
- No PTY without a grant: stub the daemon to deny, confirm no terminal appears.
- Kill the PTY host mid-session → startFailed/exited reported, grant reclaimed.
- python core/test_auth.py still 22/22 with the Console connected.

Performance — record real numbers, don't assume:
- Cold start, idle RAM, GPU probe result (webgl or dom).
- Keystroke → glyph p95 vs the 16 ms / 50 ms budget.
- dir /s in a large tree without freezing the UI.
- Ctrl+C interrupts ping -t; closing the window frees the process tree.

Continuous: npm test (71 tests) stays green; npm run contract:check passes.

---
7b. Install lock — shared node_modules with the Orb session

The root node_modules and package-lock.json are shared. Two concurrent npm installs against one tree corrupt it, and on this connection recovery means re-downloading.

Before every install step, in order:
1. tasklist for node.exe — any running process means stop and wait.
2. Check for node_modules/.staging — present means an install is mid-flight.
3. Compare package-lock.json mtime against the clock; anything inside ~2 minutes means check again before proceeding.
4. Report what I see, then install.

Prefer npm install --workspace @zoey/console to limit the blast radius, and never npm ci (it deletes the whole shared tree).

What it confirmed and could not break (worth keeping, since it was independently verified): the Node-API reasoning holds — node-pty 1.1.0 uses node-addon-api ^7, not nan, and @lydell/node-pty-win32-x64@1.1.0 ships exactly one conpty.node with no per-ABI directories, which would be impossible for a NAN addon. All six platform packages carry os/cpu gates, so npm installs only win32-x64 — no six-platform metered blowout. Choosing 1.1.0 stable over the beta is right, and vite@7 with electron-vite@5 is correct.

 * Hard-coded CSS colour.

 * Two refinements over the naive /#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}/, both added after it
 * produced false positives on GitHub issue references like `electron#20550`

 *

 *   {3}|{4}|{6}|{8}    only VALID CSS hex lengths; 5- and 7-digit runs like

 */

const PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000
const SHUTDOWN_GRACE_MS = 1_500

/**
 * The live host, tracked so it can be torn down on quit.
 *
 * This matters more on Windows than it looks. Killing the host process does NOT
 * reap the PTY's children: `cmd.exe` and its `conhost.exe` survive as orphans.
 * Verified during Step 2 — a force-kill of Electron left 2 cmd.exe and 4
 * conhost.exe behind. The shell must be told to die, in order, before the host
 * goes away.
 */
let activeChild: UtilityProcess | null = null

function log(msg: string): void {
  console.log(`[pty-host] ${msg}`)
}

/**
 * Graceful teardown: ask the host to kill its PTY, give it a moment, then kill
 * the host itself. Idempotent and safe to call when nothing is running.
 */
export async function shutdownPtyHost(): Promise<void> {
  const child = activeChild
  if (!child) return
  activeChild = null

  try {
    child.postMessage({ t: 'shutdown' })
  } catch {
    // Host already gone; fall through to the kill below.
  }

  await new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
    const timer = setTimeout(resolve, SHUTDOWN_GRACE_MS)
    child.once('exit', () => {
      clearTimeout(timer)
      resolve()
    })
  })

  try {
    child.kill()
  } catch {
    /* already exited */
  }
  log('host torn down')
}

/**
 * Fork the utilityProcess and wait for its Worker probe verdict.
 *
 * Resolves with the child and the verdict. The child is NOT killed on a failed

  Ran 1 shell command

Now hook teardown into the app lifecycle and clean up the existing orphans.

/**
 * Reap the PTY before quitting.
 *
 * `before-quit` fires while there is still an event loop to work with, so the
 * host can be told to kill its shell in order. Without this, cmd.exe and its
 * conhost.exe orphan — measured, not theorised: an ungraceful kill during Step 2
 * left 2 cmd.exe and 4 conhost.exe running.
 *
 * The one-shot guard is required because calling app.quit() from inside a
 * before-quit handler re-enters it.
 */
let teardownStarted = false
app.on('before-quit', (event) => {
  if (teardownStarted) return
  teardownStarted = true
  event.preventDefault()
  void shutdownPtyHost().finally(() => app.quit())
})

app.on('window-all-closed', () => {
  // Windows/Linux convention. The daemon's lifecycle is separate and is dealt
  // with in Step 4 — closing the Console must never kill a daemon it did not

Stock Electron 43's utilityProcess can host worker_threads. The red-team's blocker — that node-pty's Windows conout Worker might throw there — does not bite on this machine. Rung 1 holds; no drop to the main-process or ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE fallbacks. The ladder stays in src/shared/pty-ipc.ts as unused insurance, and because the MessagePort boundary is identical across all three rungs, a future drop is a config change rather than a rewrite.

Externalization confirmed in the built output, not assumed: out/main/pty-host.js contains require("@lydell/node-pty") (external ✓), out/main/index.js has zero runtime requires of @zoey/tokens (bundled ✓).

Three bugs, and one was in my own tooling

port.start is not a function — the real finding. A MessagePort cannot cross the contextIsolation boundary via contextBridge; it arrives as an inert proxy. The supported route is window.postMessage(channel, '*', event.ports) from the preload. This widens nothing — the renderer still cannot name a shell, cwd, or argv.

Cannot access 'start' before initialization — a useEffect referencing a useCallback declared below it. TypeScript caught it as TS2448 at the same time.

ptyEcho=MISSING — a false alarm from my verification code, not the transport: a setTimeout closure captured out as ''. Fixed with a ref. Worth having distinguished rather than chasing a phantom transport bug.

My contract checker cried wolf. It flagged GitHub issue refs (electron#20550, #18540) as hard-coded colours — #[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8} matches them. Fixed with a negative lookbehind and valid CSS hex lengths only, plus a 9-case regex test. One honest limitation remains by design: a bare PR #182631 is genuinely indistinguishable from a colour and will still flag, so issue refs should be written qualified.

Process-tree teardown — and a measurement I had to redo

My first teardown test was unsound: it reported "no cmd.exe during" while the shell was demonstrably alive, so it proved nothing. Re-run against the specific PID: ptyReady pid=4612 → tasklist confirms 4612 is cmd.exe → graceful close (WM_CLOSE, no /F) → 4612 gone, electron.exe 0.

This needed real code. On Windows, killing the host does not reap the PTY's children — an earlier force-kill left 2 cmd.exe + 4 conhost.exe orphaned. Now a shutdown control message makes the host kill its shell before exiting, driven by a one-shot before-quit handler.

     ---
     1. Context

     Phase 0 is done and verified: the monorepo, the frozen protocol, generated tokens and enums, and a Python daemon whose auth boundary is proven by 71 passing tests (28 protocol · 21 anti-drift · 22 live auth) plus a freshness check that fails the build on stale generated output.

     Phase 1a builds the first surface: an Electron window with one working terminal. Nothing else — no tabs, no file tree, no chat, no blocks.

     Exit criterion (yours): npm install, pip install, git, and claude all run correctly in that terminal.

     Two things make this harder than "embed xterm":

     1. No MSVC. If a native module fails to load, there is no compiler to rebuild it with. The PTY layer must be prebuilt-only, and that must be proven before ~250 MB of Electron is downloaded over a metered connection.
     2. CONTRACT §6.5 — no PTY may exist without a daemon grant. The terminal is not a local concern; it is authorized, audited, and revocable by Zoey Core.

     ⚠️ Daemon auto-start is a development stopgap

     It must be labelled as such in src/main/daemon.ts and here, because the Orb session polls for the daemon rather than spawning it. If the Console owns the daemon's lifecycle, closing the Console kills the Orb's connection — a cross-surface coupling neither contract nor spec sanctions.

     Rules:
     - Gated on process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production' / !app.isPackaged. It must never run in a packaged build.
     - If a live daemon already exists (runtime.json with a live pid), attach — never spawn a second one.
     - On Console exit, only kill a daemon this process started. Never kill an inherited one.
     - Phase 3 replaces this with a Windows service (spec §9). This code is expected to be deleted.
