     Not installing: electron-builder (Phase 5) · JetBrains Mono (1b) · the Microsoft.Windows.Console.ConPTY redistributable (1b — in-box ConPTY is adequate for one session; the redistributable's ~12 MB and its OpenConsole.exe AV-allowlist problem aren't worth it yet).

     ---
     5. Build order — cheapest risk first

     Deliberately ordered to protect metered data: the make-or-break native-module question is answered for ~2 MB, before Electron's ~250 MB is touched.

     Step 0 — Smoke test, NOT the hard gate ⚠️ (corrected by the red-team)

     My original framing was wrong. I called this the hard gate; it cannot be. Node-API is ABI-stable, so a plain-Node load essentially cannot fail for ABI reasons — and it cannot detect the one failure mode that actually threatens this design.

     The real risk: on Windows, @lydell/node-pty constructs a worker_threads Worker for its conout connection — unconditionally, for every PTY (windowsConoutConnection.js). Electron's utilityProcess has historically thrown on new Worker() because of its V8 platform (electron#20550, #18540, #26816); the fix in #20416 covered only ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE. VS Code did move its pty host to utilityProcess (vscode#175335 → PR #182631), but VS Code ships a patched Electron — strong evidence, not proof, for stock Electron 43.

     So Step 0 stays (it is ~2 MB and does prove the binary loads and the platform optional-dep resolved), but it is demoted to a smoke test. It also gains a ConPTY sanity check on this specific 22631 build: spawn cmd.exe, resize the pty, read output, and watch for hangs or assertions rather than merely "it loaded".

     The hard gate moves to the first thing in Step 2: inside the Electron utilityProcess, before any node-pty code, construct a trivial worker_threads Worker. If it throws, node-pty cannot be hosted there.

     Fallback ladder, pre-committed now so a failed probe is a config switch and not a rewrite:
     utilityProcess → main process (worker_threads confirmed working) → child_process.fork with ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 (what VS Code used for years).
     The MessagePort boundary to the renderer stays identical in all three, so the host is swappable without touching the terminal.

     Step 0 (original framing, retained for the binary check)

     Install @lydell/node-pty@1.1.0 alone. From plain Node, spawn cmd.exe, write echo hello, read bytes back, confirm exit code.
     - Pass → proceed. Fail → stop and report; the architecture changes and no Electron download is wasted.

     ⚠️ This gate is necessary but NOT sufficient, and I want to be precise about why. node-pty 1.x builds on node-addon-api@^7, i.e. Node-API, which is designed to be ABI-stable across Node versions and across other VMs including Electron — that is the whole premise of shipping one prebuilt binary. In practice N-API modules do load in Electron without electron-rebuild.

     But I could not find an unambiguous primary-source statement confirming it: Electron's own "Native Node Modules" page does not mention N-API at all, and says only that native modules "need to be recompiled for Electron" because of its different ABI — guidance aimed at old-style node-ABI addons. So the claim rests on design intent plus common practice, not on a citation.

     Therefore Step 2 re-proves it inside Electron, and that is the real gate. If it fails there, the fallback is node-pty's own prebuild download path, and failing that, hosting the PTY in a small Node sidecar outside Electron. Neither costs the Electron download twice.

     Step 1 — Electron shell

     Install Electron + electron-vite + vite@7 + React. Blank window with contextIsolation: true, nodeIntegration: false, sandbox: true.
     Measure and record: cold start, idle RAM, on this actual machine.

     Config constraints, verified 🟢 — each fails on first run if missed:

     1. Use build.externalizeDeps, NOT externalizeDepsPlugin. electron-vite's own docs: "Before electron-vite 5, you should configure it using the externalizeDepsPlugin." The plugin is the legacy API and nearly every template online still shows it. ⚠️ My earlier draft had this wrong, and apps/orb/package.json's comment references the plugin too — worth passing to the Orb session, though their mechanism is right.
     2. Dependency class is the externalization switch. electron-vite externalizes dependencies and bundles devDependencies. So:
       - @lydell/node-pty and ws → dependencies (externalized, required at runtime).
       - @zoey/protocol and @zoey/tokens → devDependencies (bundled). @zoey/protocol's entry is "main": "./src/index.ts" — raw TypeScript, noEmit, importing ./enums.generated.ts with the extension. Externalized, Electron's Node would require a .ts file and crash on launch. The Orb session reached this identical conclusion independently and documented it in their package.json.
     3. @lydell/node-pty goes in apps/console/package.json under dependencies, exact pin — not the root, not devDependencies. It resolves its binary through a runtime dynamic require plus an on-disk package.json read, so bundling it is fatal. Add build.rollupOptions.external: ['@lydell/node-pty'] as belt-and-braces.
     4. No "type": "module" in apps/console/package.json. A sandboxed Electron preload cannot be an ES module — declaring the package ESM makes electron-vite emit ESM for main and preload, and the preload then fails to load. Source stays ESM TypeScript; electron-vite transpiles main/preload to CJS. (Credit: the Orb session found this; I did not have it.)
     5. sandbox: true is compatible with the preload plan. Electron's docs confirm a sandboxed preload keeps contextBridge and ipcRenderer and may only require electron, events, timers, url. Ours needs nothing more — meaning the preload can never require('ws') or touch the filesystem, which is exactly the property we want.
     6. No packager dependency in 1a. @lydell/node-pty's asar workaround only rewrites VS Code's node_modules.asar layout and is a silent no-op for a standard app.asar — which would break its conout worker. Phase 1a's exit criterion is dev-mode only, so the cheapest correct answer is to add no packager at all and settle asar: false vs asarUnpack in Phase 5.

     Step 2 — PTY in a utilityProcess

     src/pty-host/index.ts hosts @lydell/node-pty. Main spawns it via utilityProcess.fork() and hands the renderer a MessagePort. Bytes flow PTY → utilityProcess → MessagePort → renderer, never through main, never through Python (CONTRACT §4.2).
     A native crash kills one tab's backend, not the window.

     Step 3 — xterm + renderer ladder

     @xterm/xterm + addon-fit. Pre-flight GPU probe before term.open(); attach addon-webgl only if it passes, else DOM.
     Measure keystroke → glyph against the spec §4 budget: p95 16 ms, hard fail 50 ms. Record the real number, including on the DOM path.

     Step 4 — Wire the daemon (this is what makes it legal)

     - src/main/token.ts — read %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json; ignore a stale file whose pid is dead; never cache the token to disk.
     - src/main/daemon.ts — if no live daemon, spawn python core/server.py, wait for the handshake, supervise it.
     - src/main/ws-client.ts — ws with origin: 'zoey://console', cmd.hello inside 3 s, reconnect re-reads runtime.json because the token rotates every launch.
     - cmd.pty.requestSpawn → res.pty.grant before a single PTY is created. Handle err.permission.pending (approval card) and err.permission.denied.
     - cmd.pty.report on started / exited / titleChanged / cwdChanged / startFailed — the last reclaims a dangling grant, which is exactly why it was added to the enum.
     - Honour evt.pty.revoke.

     Step 5 — Verify the exit criterion

     See §7.

     ---
     6. Files created (all under apps/console/, per STRUCTURE v2.2)

     apps\console\
     ├── package.json
     ├── electron.vite.config.ts        three configs; native deps externalized
     ├── tsconfig.json                  extends root; separate main/renderer libs
     ├── src\main\

     │   ├── token.ts                   runtime.json + stale-pid check
     │   ├── daemon.ts                  auto-start + supervise Zoey Core
     │   ├── ws-client.ts               THE only socket. Sets Origin.
     │   ├── pty-host.ts                utilityProcess lifecycle + MessagePort handoff
     │   └── grants.ts                  requestSpawn -> grant -> report -> revoke
     ├── src\pty-host\index.ts          @lydell/node-pty; bytes -> MessagePort
     ├── src\preload\index.ts           contextBridge only; no token, no socket

     Reused, not rewritten: @zoey/protocol for envelope/ULID/enums/parseDeepLink, and packages/tokens for every colour.

     ---
     7. Verification

     Exit criterion — run each in the Console's own terminal:
     - npm install in a scratch dir under C:\dev
     - npm install -g <small pkg> → lands in C:\Users\SERIOUS-PC\AppData\Roaming\npm\node_modules (this is the Session 0 / APPDATA trap; assert the daemon is not LocalSystem)
     - pip install --user <small pkg>
     - git status / git log in C:\dev\zoey
     - claude — full TUI: alt-screen, resize, colour, Ctrl+C. The hardest of the five.

     Security — must not regress Phase 0:
     - Renderer cannot reach the token or open a socket (check via devtools console).
     - No PTY without a grant: stub the daemon to deny, confirm no terminal appears.
     - Kill the PTY host mid-session → startFailed/exited reported, grant reclaimed.
     - python core/test_auth.py still 22/22 with the Console connected.

     Performance — record real numbers, don't assume:
     - Cold start, idle RAM, GPU probe result (webgl or dom).
     - Keystroke → glyph p95 vs the 16 ms / 50 ms budget.
     - dir /s in a large tree without freezing the UI.
     - Ctrl+C interrupts ping -t; closing the window frees the process tree.

     Continuous: npm test (71 tests) stays green; npm run contract:check passes.

     ---
     7b. Install lock — shared node_modules with the Orb session

     The root node_modules and package-lock.json are shared. Two concurrent npm installs against one tree corrupt it, and on this connection recovery means re-downloading.

     Before every install step, in order:
     1. tasklist for node.exe — any running process means stop and wait.
     2. Check for node_modules/.staging — present means an install is mid-flight.
     3. Compare package-lock.json mtime against the clock; anything inside ~2 minutes means check again before proceeding.
     4. Report what I see, then install.

     Prefer npm install --workspace @zoey/console to limit the blast radius, and never npm ci (it deletes the whole shared tree).

Process-tree teardown — proven against a real PID

The plan requires closing the window to free the process tree. Measured properly:
ptyReady pid=4612 → tasklist confirms 4612 is cmd.exe → graceful close (WM_CLOSE, no /F) → 4612 gone, electron.exe 0.

An earlier attempt at this test was unsound — it reported "no cmd.exe" while the shell was demonstrably alive, so it proved nothing. Re-run against the specific PID.

Why teardown needed code at all: on Windows, killing the host does not reap the PTY's children. A force-kill during Step 2 left 2 cmd.exe + 4 conhost.exe orphaned. Fixed with a shutdown control message (host kills the shell, then exits) plus a one-shot before-quit handler in main.

My own contract checker had a false positive

scripts/check-contract.mjs flagged GitHub issue refs (electron#20550, #18540) as hard-coded colours — the regex #[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8} matches them. Fixed with a negative lookbehind for identifier chars and valid CSS hex lengths only (3/4/6/8), plus a 9-case regex test. A checker that cries wolf trains you to ignore it.

Known limit, by design: a bare PR #182631 is genuinely indistinguishable from a colour and will still flag. Write issue refs qualified (electron/electron#182631).

▎ Supersedes the Phase 0 plan (complete). Authority order: CONTRACT.md → docs/STRUCTURE.md (v2.2) → docs/ZOEY_OS-spec.md (v3.2) → this file.

---
1. Context

Phase 0 is done and verified: the monorepo, the frozen protocol, generated tokens and enums, and a Python daemon whose auth boundary is proven by 71 passing tests (28 protocol · 21 anti-drift · 22 live auth) plus a freshness check that fails the build on stale generated output.

Phase 1a builds the first surface: an Electron window with one working terminal. Nothing else — no tabs, no file tree, no chat, no blocks.

Exit criterion (yours): npm install, pip install, git, and claude all run correctly in that terminal.

Two things make this harder than "embed xterm":

1. No MSVC. If a native module fails to load, there is no compiler to rebuild it with. The PTY layer must be prebuilt-only, and that must be proven before ~250 MB of Electron is downloaded over a metered connection.
2. CONTRACT §6.5 — no PTY may exist without a daemon grant. The terminal is not a local concern; it is authorized, audited, and revocable by Zoey Core.

⚠️ Daemon auto-start is a development stopgap

It must be labelled as such in src/main/daemon.ts and here, because the Orb session polls for the daemon rather than spawning it. If the Console owns the daemon's lifecycle, closing the Console kills the Orb's connection — a cross-surface coupling neither contract nor spec sanctions.

Rules:
- Gated on process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production' / !app.isPackaged. It must never run in a packaged build.
- If a live daemon already exists (runtime.json with a live pid), attach — never spawn a second one.
- On Console exit, only kill a daemon this process started. Never kill an inherited one.
- Phase 3 replaces this with a Windows service (spec §9). This code is expected to be deleted.

---
5. Build order — cheapest risk first

Deliberately ordered to protect metered data: the make-or-break native-module question is answered for ~2 MB, before Electron's ~250 MB is touched.

Step 0 — Smoke test, NOT the hard gate ⚠️ (corrected by the red-team)

My original framing was wrong. I called this the hard gate; it cannot be. Node-API is ABI-stable, so a plain-Node load essentially cannot fail for ABI reasons — and it cannot detect the one failure mode that actually threatens this design.

The real risk: on Windows, @lydell/node-pty constructs a worker_threads Worker for its conout connection — unconditionally, for every PTY (windowsConoutConnection.js). Electron's utilityProcess has historically thrown on new Worker() because of its V8 platform (electron#20550, #18540, #26816); the fix in #20416 covered only ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE. VS Code did move its pty host to utilityProcess (vscode#175335 → PR #182631), but VS Code ships a patched Electron — strong evidence, not proof, for stock Electron 43.

So Step 0 stays (it is ~2 MB and does prove the binary loads and the platform optional-dep resolved), but it is demoted to a smoke test. It also gains a ConPTY sanity check on this specific 22631 build: spawn cmd.exe, resize the pty, read output, and watch for hangs or assertions rather than merely "it loaded".

The hard gate moves to the first thing in Step 2: inside the Electron utilityProcess, before any node-pty code, construct a trivial worker_threads Worker. If it throws, node-pty cannot be hosted there.

Fallback ladder, pre-committed now so a failed probe is a config switch and not a rewrite:
utilityProcess → main process (worker_threads confirmed working) → child_process.fork with ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 (what VS Code used for years).
The MessagePort boundary to the renderer stays identical in all three, so the host is swappable without touching the terminal.

Step 0 (original framing, retained for the binary check)

Install @lydell/node-pty@1.1.0 alone. From plain Node, spawn cmd.exe, write echo hello, read bytes back, confirm exit code.
- Pass → proceed. Fail → stop and report; the architecture changes and no Electron download is wasted.

⚠️ This gate is necessary but NOT sufficient, and I want to be precise about why. node-pty 1.x builds on node-addon-api@^7, i.e. Node-API, which is designed to be ABI-stable across Node versions and across other VMs including Electron — that is the whole premise of shipping one prebuilt binary. In practice N-API modules do load in Electron without electron-rebuild.

But I could not find an unambiguous primary-source statement confirming it: Electron's own "Native Node Modules" page does not mention N-API at all, and says only that native modules "need to be recompiled for Electron" because of its different ABI — guidance aimed at old-style node-ABI addons. So the claim rests on design intent plus common practice, not on a citation.

Therefore Step 2 re-proves it inside Electron, and that is the real gate. If it fails there, the fallback is node-pty's own prebuild download path, and failing that, hosting the PTY in a small Node sidecar outside Electron. Neither costs the Electron download twice.

Step 1 — Electron shell

Install Electron + electron-vite + vite@7 + React. Blank window with contextIsolation: true, nodeIntegration: false, sandbox: true.
Measure and record: cold start, idle RAM, on this actual machine.

Config constraints, verified 🟢 — each fails on first run if missed:

1. Use build.externalizeDeps, NOT externalizeDepsPlugin. electron-vite's own docs: "Before electron-vite 5, you should configure it using the externalizeDepsPlugin." The plugin is the legacy API and nearly every template online still shows it. ⚠️ My earlier draft had this wrong, and apps/orb/package.json's comment references the plugin too — worth passing to the Orb session, though their mechanism is right.
2. Dependency class is the externalization switch. electron-vite externalizes dependencies and bundles devDependencies. So:
  - @lydell/node-pty and ws → dependencies (externalized, required at runtime).
  - @zoey/protocol and @zoey/tokens → devDependencies (bundled). @zoey/protocol's entry is "main": "./src/index.ts" — raw TypeScript, noEmit, importing ./enums.generated.ts with the extension. Externalized, Electron's Node would require a .ts file and crash on launch. The Orb session reached this identical conclusion independently and documented it in their package.json.
3. @lydell/node-pty goes in apps/console/package.json under dependencies, exact pin — not the root, not devDependencies. It resolves its binary through a runtime dynamic require plus an on-disk package.json read, so bundling it is fatal. Add build.rollupOptions.external: ['@lydell/node-pty'] as belt-and-braces.
4. No "type": "module" in apps/console/package.json. A sandboxed Electron preload cannot be an ES module — declaring the package ESM makes electron-vite emit ESM for main and preload, and the preload then fails to load. Source stays ESM TypeScript; electron-vite transpiles main/preload to CJS. (Credit: the Orb session found this; I did not have it.)
5. sandbox: true is compatible with the preload plan. Electron's docs confirm a sandboxed preload keeps contextBridge and ipcRenderer and may only require electron, events, timers, url. Ours needs nothing more — meaning the preload can never require('ws') or touch the filesystem, which is exactly the property we want.
6. No packager dependency in 1a. @lydell/node-pty's asar workaround only rewrites VS Code's node_modules.asar layout and is a silent no-op for a standard app.asar — which would break its conout worker. Phase 1a's exit criterion is dev-mode only, so the cheapest correct answer is to add no packager at all and settle asar: false vs asarUnpack in Phase 5.

Step 2 — PTY in a utilityProcess

src/pty-host/index.ts hosts @lydell/node-pty. Main spawns it via utilityProcess.fork() and hands the renderer a MessagePort. Bytes flow PTY → utilityProcess → MessagePort → renderer, never through main, never through Python (CONTRACT §4.2).
A native crash kills one tab's backend, not the window.

Step 3 — xterm + renderer ladder ← THIS TURN

New files under apps/console/src/renderer/terminal/: Terminal.tsx, gpu-probe.ts, theme.ts, latency.ts.
No new dependencies — @xterm/xterm 6.0.0, addon-fit 0.11.0, addon-webgl 0.19.0 are already installed.

Reused, not rewritten: the PtyToHost/PtyFromHost protocol and the window.postMessage port handoff from Step 2 are unchanged. xterm replaces the <pre> sink; the transport underneath it does not move.

(a) The probe — gpu-probe.ts, runs BEFORE term.open()
Throwaway canvas → getContext('webgl2') → read UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL / UNMASKED_VENDOR_WEBGL, reject on a software-renderer substring (SwiftShader / llvmpipe / Software). Returns { rung: 'webgl' | 'dom', reason } and the reason string is reported verbatim.

The Orb's live probe already showed hardware WebGL2 (ANGLE / Direct3D11, vs_5_0 ps_5_0) on this HD 620, so webgl is the expected rung — but it is probed, not assumed, and the DOM rung is forced and measured too so the only fallback xterm 6 has is proven rather than hoped for.

(b)/(c) Latency — latency.ts, both rungs
N=200 sequential single characters (one outstanding at a time, so pipelining cannot flatter the number):
t0 before port.postMessage({t:'write'}) → shell echoes → term.write(data, cb) → paint → t1.
Report p50 / p95 / max on the selected rung and again with the DOM rung forced.

▎ ⚠️ The exact "paint" instant is the part most likely to be measured wrongly — whether xterm 6's write callback fires on parse or on render, and whether one requestAnimationFrame is the true paint. That specific question is out with a 3-agent methodology review right now, and its answer lands before any number is reported. This is the same class of error as the unsound Step 2 teardown test.

Attribution, not guessing (your CPU-bound hint): the harness also records frame time and long-task duration alongside each sample. If p95 misses 16 ms, the report says which axis — React re-render per chunk, base64 decode, xterm's parser, or the MessagePort hop — rather than blaming the renderer path. The Orb's 42.8 ms frame at 0.2 ms GPU submit is the reference point.

Theming — theme.ts
xterm's ITheme built from @zoey/tokens JSON, never hex literals; check-contract.mjs fails the build on one, and it now scans 37 files.

⚠️ Gap to flag: the token set has no 16-colour ANSI ramp. Plan is to token only background / foreground / cursor / selection and keep xterm's built-in ANSI defaults, because packages/tokens is SHARED and I will not edit it. If you want the ANSI ramp tokened, that is a packages/tokens diff to propose to you — not something I'll slip in.

(d)/(e)/(f) The remaining measurements
- dir /s over a large tree — UI responsiveness, measured as dropped frames / max frame gap, not eyeballed
- \x03 interrupts ping -t and the shell returns to a prompt
- Window close frees the whole process tree — verified BY PID, the Step 2 method, never by process name

Untouched: the missing-daemon-grant comment on zoey:pty-start in src/main/index.ts stays exactly as written. Step 4 closes it.

Step 4 — Wire the daemon (this is what makes it legal)

- src/main/token.ts — read %LOCALAPPDATA%\Zoey\runtime.json; ignore a stale file whose pid is dead; never cache the token to disk.
- src/main/daemon.ts — if no live daemon, spawn python core/server.py, wait for the handshake, supervise it.
- src/main/ws-client.ts — ws with origin: 'zoey://console', cmd.hello inside 3 s, reconnect re-reads runtime.json because the token rotates every launch.
- cmd.pty.requestSpawn → res.pty.grant before a single PTY is created. Handle err.permission.pending (approval card) and err.permission.denied.
- cmd.pty.report on started / exited / titleChanged / cwdChanged / startFailed — the last reclaims a dangling grant, which is exactly why it was added to the enum.
- Honour evt.pty.revoke.

Step 5 — Verify the exit criterion

See §7.

7. Verification

Exit criterion — run each in the Console's own terminal:
- npm install in a scratch dir under C:\dev
- npm install -g <small pkg> → lands in C:\Users\SERIOUS-PC\AppData\Roaming\npm\node_modules (this is the Session 0 / APPDATA trap; assert the daemon is not LocalSystem)
- pip install --user <small pkg>
- git status / git log in C:\dev\zoey
- claude — full TUI: alt-screen, resize, colour, Ctrl+C. The hardest of the five.

Security — must not regress Phase 0:
- Renderer cannot reach the token or open a socket (check via devtools console).
- No PTY without a grant: stub the daemon to deny, confirm no terminal appears.
- Kill the PTY host mid-session → startFailed/exited reported, grant reclaimed.
- python core/test_auth.py still 22/22 with the Console connected.

Performance — record real numbers, don't assume:
- Cold start, idle RAM, GPU probe result (webgl or dom).
- Keystroke → glyph p95 vs the 16 ms / 50 ms budget.
- dir /s in a large tree without freezing the UI.
- Ctrl+C interrupts ping -t; closing the window frees the process tree.

Continuous: npm test (71 tests) stays green; npm run contract:check passes.

---
7b. Install lock — shared node_modules with the Orb session

The root node_modules and package-lock.json are shared. Two concurrent npm installs against one tree corrupt it, and on this connection recovery means re-downloading.

Before every install step, in order:
1. tasklist for node.exe — any running process means stop and wait.
2. Check for node_modules/.staging — present means an install is mid-flight.
3. Compare package-lock.json mtime against the clock; anything inside ~2 minutes means check again before proceeding.
4. Report what I see, then install.

Prefer npm install --workspace @zoey/console to limit the blast radius, and never npm ci (it deletes the whole shared tree).

┌─────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  #  │                                                  Finding                                                   │                Where fixed                 │
├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1   │ Step 0 gates the wrong thing — Node-API can't fail for ABI reasons, and plain Node can't see node-pty's    │ §5 Step 0 rewritten; real gate moved into  │
│     │ worker_threads use                                                                                         │ Step 2                                     │
├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 2   │ Fallback ladder must be pre-committed so a failed probe is a switch, not a rewrite                         │ §5 Step 0                                  │
├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 3   │ @lydell/node-pty must sit in apps/console dependencies, exact pin                                          │ §5 Step 1, constraint 3                    │
├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 4   │ externalizeDepsPlugin is the pre-v5 API; v5 uses build.externalizeDeps                                     │ §5 Step 1, constraint 1                    │
├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 5   │ asar would silently break node-pty's conout worker                                                         │ §5 Step 1, constraint 6                    │
└─────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┘

What it confirmed and could not break (worth keeping, since it was independently verified): the Node-API reasoning holds — node-pty 1.1.0 uses node-addon-api ^7, not nan, and @lydell/node-pty-win32-x64@1.1.0 ships exactly one conpty.node with no per-ABI directories, which would be impossible for a NAN addon. All six platform packages carry os/cpu gates, so npm installs only win32-x64 — no six-platform metered blowout. Choosing 1.1.0 stable over the beta is right, and vite@7 with electron-vite@5 is correct.

    //
    // `cur` is hoisted because `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` types `number[]`
    // indexing as `number | undefined`. The guard is unreachable in practice —
    // `i` is bounded by the array's own length — but narrowing it here keeps
    // the carry logic identical rather than papering over it with `!`.

      const cur = lastUlidRandom[i];
      if (cur === undefined) continue;
      if (cur < 31) { lastUlidRandom[i] = cur + 1; break; }

  // the echo assertion captures out='' at effect time and always reports MISSING.
  const outRef = useRef<string>('')

  const nodeLeaks = (['require', 'process', 'module', 'global', 'Buffer'] as const).filter(
    (k) => k in globalThis,

        ? 'SELFCHECK nodeAccess=none (sandboxed)'
        : `SELFCHECK nodeAccess=LEAKED ${nodeLeaks.join(',')}`,
    )

    // The port arrives via window.postMessage, not contextBridge — a
    // MessagePort cannot cross the isolated-world boundary as a bridged value.
    const onWindowMessage = (e: MessageEvent): void => {
      if (e.data !== PTY_PORT_CHANNEL) return
      const port = e.ports[0]
      if (!port) return
      portRef.current = port
      port.onmessage = (ev: MessageEvent) => {
        const m = ev.data as PtyFromHost
        switch (m.t) {
          case 'ready':
            console.log(`SELFCHECK ptyReady pid=${m.pid}`)
            break
          case 'data': {
            const chunk = atob(m.b64)
            outRef.current = (outRef.current + chunk).slice(-4000)
            setOut(outRef.current)
            break
          }
          case 'exit':
            setOut((prev) => `${prev}\n[exited code=${m.code}]`)
            console.log(`SELFCHECK ptyExit code=${m.code}`)
            break
          case 'error':
            setOut((prev) => `${prev}\n[error ${m.message}]`)
            break
        }
      }
      port.start()
      console.log('SELFCHECK ptyPort=received')
    }

    window.addEventListener('message', onWindowMessage)
    return () => window.removeEventListener('message', onWindowMessage)
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [])

  useEffect(() => {
    if (preRef.current) preRef.current.scrollTop = preRef.current.scrollHeight
  }, [out])

  const send = (m: PtyToHost): void => portRef.current?.postMessage(m)

  const start = useCallback(async () => {
    setHost('starting…')
    const r = await window.zoey.startPty({ cols: 100, rows: 24 })
    if (r.ok) {
      setHost(`${r.kind}${r.pid ? ` pid=${r.pid}` : ''}`)
      setProbe(`worker_threads PASSED (${r.probeMs.toFixed(0)} ms)`)
      console.log(`SELFCHECK ptyHost=${r.kind} workerOk=${r.workerOk}`)
    } else {
      setHost('FAILED')
      setProbe(r.error)
      console.log(`SELFCHECK ptyHost=failed ${r.error}`)
    }
  }, [])

  const runEcho = (): void => send({ t: 'write', b64: btoa('echo ZOEY_STEP2_OK\r') })

  // Self-driving verification so the probe, spawn, echo and resize can be
  // checked from a log without a human clicking. Declared HERE, after `start`
  // and `send` exist — placing it above them is a temporal-dead-zone crash that
  // TypeScript catches as TS2448 and React reports as "Cannot access 'start'
  // before initialization". Removed in Step 3, when xterm owns the session.
  useEffect(() => {
    const t = setTimeout(() => void start(), 600)
    return () => clearTimeout(t)
  }, [start])

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!host.startsWith('utilityProcess')) return
    const a = setTimeout(runEcho, 700)
    const b = setTimeout(() => send({ t: 'resize', cols: 120, rows: 40 }), 1400)
    const c = setTimeout(
      () =>
        console.log(
          outRef.current.includes('ZOEY_STEP2_OK')
            ? 'SELFCHECK ptyEcho=ok'
            : `SELFCHECK ptyEcho=MISSING (${outRef.current.length} bytes seen)`,
        ),
      2400,
    )
    return () => {
      clearTimeout(a)
      clearTimeout(b)
      clearTimeout(c)
    }
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [host])

  return (
    <main className="shell">
      <h1 className="brand">ZOEY CONSOLE</h1>
      <p className="phase">PHASE 1A · STEP 2 — PTY OVER MESSAGEPORT</p>

      <dl className="facts">
        <dt>contextBridge</dt>
        <dd data-state={bridge}>{bridge}</dd>

        <dt>renderer node access</dt>
        <dd data-state={nodeLeaks.length === 0 ? 'ok' : 'failed'}>
          {nodeLeaks.length === 0 ? 'none — sandboxed' : `LEAKED: ${nodeLeaks.join(', ')}`}
        </dd>

        <dt>pty host</dt>
        <dd data-state={host.startsWith('utilityProcess') ? 'ok' : host === 'FAILED' ? 'failed' : 'checking'}>
          {host}
        </dd>

        <dt>worker probe</dt>
        <dd>{probe}</dd>
      </dl>

      <div className="row">
        <button onClick={start}>start pty</button>
        <button onClick={runEcho}>echo</button>
        <button onClick={() => send({ t: 'resize', cols: 120, rows: 40 })}>resize</button>
        <button onClick={() => send({ t: 'kill' })}>kill</button>
      </div>

      <pre className="out" ref={preRef}>
        {out || '(no output yet — press “start pty”)'}
      </pre>
      <header className="top">
        <h1 className="brand">ZOEY CONSOLE</h1>
        <span className="phase">
          PHASE 1A · STEP 3 · bridge <b data-state={bridge}>{bridge}</b> · node{' '}
          <b data-state={nodeLeaks.length === 0 ? 'ok' : 'failed'}>
            {nodeLeaks.length === 0 ? 'sandboxed' : 'LEAKED'}
          </b>
        </span>
      </header>
      <TerminalView />
    </main>
  )

  // Measurement flags are forwarded from argv into the renderer's query string
  // so the latency harness can be driven from a script instead of a click.
  // `--force-dom` proves the DOM fallback rung; `--measure` auto-runs the
  // harness once the PTY is ready. Neither is set on a normal launch, so a
  // normal launch carries none of the harness's overhead.
  const flags: string[] = []
  if (process.argv.includes('--force-dom')) flags.push('forceDom')
  if (process.argv.includes('--measure')) flags.push('measure')
  const search = flags.join('&')

  if (isDev && process.env['ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL']) {
    void win.loadURL(process.env['ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL'])
    const url = new URL(process.env['ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL'])
    if (search) url.search = search
    void win.loadURL(url.toString())
  } else {
    void win.loadFile(join(__dirname, '../renderer/index.html'))
    void win.loadFile(join(__dirname, '../renderer/index.html'), search ? { search } : undefined)
  }

  return win

      // No remote module, no node in workers.

● Update(apps\console\src\main\index.ts)
Added 20 lines

const isDev = !app.isPackaged

/**
 * Keep painting when the window is covered.
 *
 * Windows has a native occlusion detector: when another window fully covers
 * ours, Chromium stops compositing the renderer entirely — `requestAnimationFrame`
 * simply never fires. `backgroundThrottling: false` does NOT cover this; it
 * relaxes timer throttling, not occlusion. The symptom was a latency run that
 * stalled with `frameStalls=20` and no error, on a window that had definitely
 * shown (`ready-to-show` had fired and logged).
 *
 * These must be appended BEFORE `app.whenReady()`.
 *
 * This is not only a measurement concern: a terminal streaming a long build
 * behind another window must keep rendering, or the scrollback lurches when you
 * bring it back to the front.
 */
app.commandLine.appendSwitch('disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows')
app.commandLine.appendSwitch('disable-renderer-backgrounding')
app.commandLine.appendSwitch('disable-features', 'CalculateNativeWinOcclusion')

/** Cold-start measurement — reported at first paint, not guessed. */
const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint()
const msSince = (from: bigint): number => Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - from) / 1e6

2. Step 3

(a) Rung and reason — answered, reproducible across every run:
webgl — hardware webgl2 — ANGLE (Intel, Intel(R) HD Graphics 620 (0x00005916) Direct3D11 vs_5_0 ps_5_0, D3D11)
webglAddon=attached
Matches the Orb's finding exactly. The probe runs before term.open() and uses failIfMajorPerformanceCaveat so Chromium itself refuses a software-backed context.

(f) Process tree — verified by PID, not by name:
ptyReady pid=4628 → Win32_Process confirms 4628 is cmd.exe → graceful CloseMainWindow() → pid gone, electron 0, [pty-host] host torn down logged.

(b), (c), (d), (e) — I did not get these, and I'm not going to dress that up.

The latency harness aborts with frameStalls=20, n=0. It correctly refuses to emit a number rather than fabricate one — that part works — but it isn't producing measurements. I chased four distinct causes and fixed all four; none was the last one:

1. Chromium throttles timers/rAF in a backgrounded window → backgroundThrottling: false. Real: took the run from ~1 sample/sec to ~20/sec.
2. afterPaint() leaked a MessageChannel per sample (~2000 unclosed ports over a run) → closed both ends.
3. Timed-out echo waiters were never deregistered → stale matchers stealing later samples' chunks → added cancel().
4. afterPaint() cleared its own watchdog before settling → a missed message wedged it with no timeout left → watchdog now clears only on settle.

The remaining symptom is longFrames max≈1015 ms — the main thread blocked for a full second per sample, which is not the occlusion story my abort message claims. That message is now misleading and I'd fix it first next session.

What is genuinely working: xterm renders on the WebGL rung, the PTY round-trips (tx#0 input="a" → rx#2 "a" captured in diagnostics), and an earlier build completed 600 sequential samples — so the transport and the terminal are fine. It's my measurement harness that isn't.

Findings worth keeping regardless

- term.write(data, cb) fires the callback on parse, not render — verified in WriteBuffer.ts:221-222, with _onWriteParsed.fire() at 245. Stopping a clock there omits render, paint and composite.
- _didUserInput takes a synchronous parse path (WriteBuffer.ts:115-122); without it you get an extra setTimeout macrotask real typing never pays. So input must be driven via term.input().
- Every ITheme field is optional — so tokening background/foreground/cursor/selection and keeping xterm's ANSI defaults is valid. No packages/tokens change needed, which I'd otherwise have had to propose to you.
- A */ inside a path in a block comment closes the comment early. apps/*/src in a JSDoc silently turned a file into syntax errors.

The boundary question you ruled on

Recorded in latency.ts: report both, judge on software-only. On your panel — 60 Hz, 16.70 ms measured, not assumed — the vsync wait alone is p95 ≈ 15.84 ms, so a to-photons 16 ms leaves ~0.16 ms for everything else. The harness will print both lines and a NO DATA verdict rather than a verdict on NaN.

Repo is clean: typecheck passes on all three projects, both suites green, no stray processes.
